The dimples had vanished from the doctor’s full cheeks. There was something contemptuous in the peevishness of his thin lips and the extreme hardness of his eyes. They softened somewhat before he addressed Cosmo.

“Here is another point of view for you. Devil only knows what that idea is, but I suspect it’s vague enough to include every illusion that ever fooled mankind. There must be some charm in that gray coat and that old three-cornered hat of his, for the man himself has betrayed every hatred and every hope that have helped him on his way.”

===Chinese===
科斯莫察觉到自己方才竟忘了形。「是的，」他说，「这一点无法否认。」

「是啊，」医生接着说，「而现在，离我们不出二十码，在墙的那一边，有成百万的人至今仍然爱着他。喂！坎泰洛奇！」他隔着此时已空无一人的整间餐厅喊道，「到这儿来。」

这位客栈主人——他方才一直在远处一张餐具柜旁悄悄地忙碌——恭谨地走上前来，只穿着衬衫袖子，腰间系着一条长长的围裙，围裙的一角撩了起来，头上戴着一顶白帽子。被问起意大利人是不是真的爱拿破仑，他鞠了一躬，答了一声「阁下（Excellency）」。

「你自己也觉得他是个伟人，是不是？」医生追问，又得到了一鞠躬，和又一声嘟囔着的「阁下」。

医生得意地转向科斯莫。「你瞧！而这些年来，波拿巴把他们所有能下手的东西都偷了个精光。他们所有的艺术品。我只奇怪他没把画着《最后的晚餐》（*The Last Supper*）的那面墙也一并搬走。这事叫我热血沸腾。你知道，我是热爱意大利的。」他又对着那一动不动的坎泰洛奇说起来。

「可到底是什么，叫你们这些人爱这个男人？」

这一回坎泰洛奇没有鞠躬。他似乎费了点力气：「先生（Signore），是那个理想（idea）。」

医生默默地把目光重新转向科斯莫。最后，这位客栈主人在离开他这两位英国主顾转身之前，后退了三步。医生那饱满脸颊上的酒窝已经不见了。他那薄嘴唇愠怒的样子、那一双眼睛的极端冷硬里，透出某种轻蔑。在他开口对科斯莫说话之前，那眼神柔和了一些。

「这又是一种给你看的看法。只有魔鬼才知道那个理想究竟是什么，可我怀疑它模糊得很，足以把曾经愚弄过人类的每一种幻象都囊括其中。他身上那件灰外套、那顶旧三角帽里，想必有某种魔力，因为他这个人，凡是帮过他上路的每一种仇恨、每一种希望，统统都背叛过。」

===Original===
“What I am wondering at,” Cosmo said at last, “is whether you have ever talked like this to anybody before.”

The doctor seemed taken aback a little.

“Oh. You mean about Bonaparte,” he said. “If you had gone to that other inn, Pollegrini’s, more suitable to your nationality and social position, you would have heard nothing of that kind. I am not very communicative really, but to sit at meals like two mutes would have been impossible. What could we have conversed about? One must have some subject other than the weather and, frankly, what other subject would we have had here in Genoa, or for that matter in any other spot of the civilized world? I know there are amongst us in England a good many young men who call themselves revolutionists and even republicans. Charming young men, generous and all that. Friends of Boney. You might be one of them.”

As he paused markedly Cosmo murmured that he was hardly prepared to state what he was. That other inn, the Pollegrini, was full when he arrived.

“Well, there had been three departures this morning,” the doctor informed him. “You can have your things packed up this afternoon and carried across the Place. You know, by staying here you make yourself conspicuous to the spies, not to speak of the thieves; they ask themselves: ‘What sort of inferior Englishman is that?’ With me it is different. I am known for a man who has his own work to do. People are curious. And as my work is confidential I prefer to keep out of the way rather than have to be rude. But for you it would be more amusing to live over there. New faces all the time; endless gossip about all sorts of people.”

“I do not think it is worth while to change now,” said Cosmo coldly.

“Of course not, if you are not going to prolong your stay. If you project a visit to Elba, Livorno is the port for that. And if you are anxious to hear about Napoleon you will hear plenty of gossip about him there. Here you have nothing but my talk.”

===Chinese===
「我所惊奇的，」科斯莫终于说道，「是你从前可曾对任何人这样讲过话。」

医生似乎稍稍吃了一惊。

「哦。你是指关于波拿巴的话，」他说。「要是你去了另一家客栈，波莱格里尼（Pollegrini）家那家，更合乎你的国籍和身份的客栈，你就不会听到这种话了。我其实并不十分多嘴，可吃饭时像两个哑巴似的坐着，也是不可能的。我们能谈些什么呢？人总得有个题目，不能光谈天气，坦白说，在这儿热那亚，或者就此而言在文明世界的任何一处地方，我们还能有什么别的题目？我知道，在我们英国人当中，有不少年轻人自称革命者，甚至自称共和派。都是些讨人喜欢的年轻人，慷慨大方云云。是波尼（Boney）的朋友。说不定你就是其中之一。」

他有意顿了一顿，科斯莫便嘟囔着说，自己还不大能说得清自己究竟是怎样的人。那另一家客栈，波莱格里尼家的，他刚到的时候已经客满了。

「这个嘛，今天上午已经走了三拨客人，」医生告诉他。「你可以今天下午就把行李收拾好，叫人搬到广场对面去。你要知道，你住在这儿，是在密探面前引人注目，更不用说那些小偷了；他们心里在琢磨：『那是个什么样的次等英国人呢？』跟我则不同。我是出了名的有自己一摊事要做的人。人是好奇的。而我那摊事又须保密，我宁可躲远点，也不愿弄得非粗声粗气不可。可对你来说，住到那边去会更有趣些。总能见到生面孔；有无穷无尽关于形形色色的人的闲话。」

「我看现在换地方并不值得，」科斯莫冷冷地说。

「当然不值得，要是你不打算久留的话。要是你打算去一趟厄尔巴岛，里窝那（Livorno）才是出发的口岸。你若急于听到关于拿破仑的消息，在那边会听到一大堆关于他的闲话。在这里，你除了我这张嘴，再没别的了。」

===Original===
“I have found it very interesting,” said Cosmo, rising to go away. The doctor smiled without amiability. He was determined never to let Cosmo guess that he knew of his acquaintance with the people occupying the palace guarded by the symbolic griffins. Of that fact he had been made aware by the Count de Montevesso who, once, he had got the doctor into Clelia’s room, decided to take him into his confidence—on the ground that one must be frank with a medical man. The real reason was, however, that knowing Doctor Martel to be employed on secret political work by the statesmen of the Alliance, and having a very great idea of his occult influences in all sorts of spheres, he hoped to get from him another sort of assistance. His last words were, “You see yourself the state the child is in. I want that popinjay moved out of Genoa.”

The only answer of the doctor to this, and the last sound during that professional visit that Count de Montevesso heard from him, was a short wooden laugh. That man of political intrigues, confidential missions (often he had more than one at a time on his hands), inordinately vain of his backstairs importance, was not mercenary. He had always preserved a most independent attitude towards his employers. To him the Count de Montevesso was but a common stupid soldier of fortune of no importance and of no position except as the son-in-law of the Marquis d’Armand. He had never seen him before, but his marital life was known to him as it was known to the rest of the world. To be waylaid by a strange priest just as he was leaving the Marquis’s room was annoying enough, but he could not very well refuse the request since it seemed to be a case of sudden illness. He was soon enlightened as to its nature by Clelia, who had treated him and the Count to another of her indescribable performances. Characteristically enough the doctor had never been for a moment irritated with the girl. He behaved by her tempestuous bedside like a man of science, calm, attentive, impenetrable. But it was afterwards, when he had been drawn aside by the Count for a confidential talk, that he had asked himself whether he were dreaming or awake. His scorn for the man helped him to preserve his self-command, and to the end the Count was not intelligent enough to perceive its character.

===Chinese===
「我觉得非常有意思，」科斯莫说着，站起身要走。医生笑了笑，笑里不带一丝和气。他打定主意，绝不让科斯莫猜到他其实知道他与那座府邸里的人有来往——那座府邸由几只象征性的神鹰守着门。这一层，他是从蒙特韦索伯爵（Count de Montevesso）那里得知的；伯爵有一次设法把他引进了克莱莉娅（Clelia）的房间，便决定对他推心置腹——理由是，对一位医生总该坦诚相待。然而真正的原因却是：伯爵知道马泰尔医生正受同盟各国政治家之雇做秘密的政治工作，又极看重他在各种圈子里那种种隐秘的势力，希望从他那里得到另一种帮助。他最后的话是：「你自己也瞧得见那孩子是什么情形。我要那只花花公鸡离开热那亚。」

医生对此唯一的回答——也是那次出诊之中蒙特韦索伯爵所能从他那里听到的最后一声响——是一声短促、木然的笑。这个玩弄政治阴谋的人、身负秘密使命的人（常常同时有好几桩在手里），对自己那种幕后权势极其自矜，却并不贪财。他向来对自己的雇主们保持着一种极其独立的姿态。在他看来，蒙特韦索伯爵不过是个平平常常、愚笨的投机军人，毫无分量、毫无地位，只不过作为达蒙侯爵（Marquis d’Armand）的女婿才算有点身份。他从前从未见过此人，可此人的婚姻生活，也像世人皆知那样，为他所熟知。正当他要离开侯爵的房间时，被一个陌生的神父拦住，这已经够叫人恼火了，可他又不便拒绝这请求，因为看上去像是一桩突发的病症。很快，克莱莉娅便让他明白了这病症的性质——她又为他与伯爵表演了一出她那难以形容的把戏。颇具特征的是，医生对这姑娘竟不曾有过一瞬的恼火。在她那风暴一般的病榻之侧，他表现得像个科学家，冷静、专注、不动声色。可是在那之后，当伯爵把他拉到一旁密谈时，他都不禁自问，自己是在做梦，还是醒着。他对这个男人的鄙夷，帮他维持住了自制；而伯爵直到最后也不够聪明，竟辨不出这份鄙夷的性质。

===Original===
The doctor left the Palazzo about an hour after Cosmo (but not by the same staircase) and on his way to his inn gave rein to his indignation. Did the stupid brute imagine that he had any sort of claim on his services? Ah, he wanted that popinjay removed from Genoa! Indeed! And what the devil did he care for it? Was he expected to arrange a neat little assassination to please that solemn wooden imbecile? The doctor’s sense of self-importance was grievously hurt. Even in the morning after a good night’s rest he had not shaken off the impression. However, he was reasonable enough not to make Cosmo in any way responsible for what he defined to himself as the most incredibly offensive experience of his life. He only looked at him when he came to lunch with a sort of acid amusement as the being who had had the power to arouse a passion of love in the primitive soul of that curious little savage. As the meal proceeded, the doctor seemed to notice that his young countryman was somehow changed. He watched him covertly. What had happened to him since last evening? Surely he hadn’t been smitten himself by the little savage that under no circumstances could have been made fit to be a housemaid in an English family.

After he had been left by Cosmo alone in the dining room, the doctor’s body continued to loll in the chair while his thoughts continued to circle around that funny affair, of which you couldn’t say whether it was love at first sight or a manifestation of some inherited lunacy. Quite a good-looking young man. Out of the common too, in a distinguished way. Altogether a specimen of one’s countrymen one could well be proud of, mused further the doctor, whose tastes had been formed by much intercourse with all kinds of people. Characteristically enough, too, he felt for a moment sorry in his grumpy contemptuous way for the little dishevelled savage with a hooked nose and burning cheeks and her thin sticks of bare arms. The doctor was humane. The origin of his reputation sprang from his humanity. But his thought, as soon as it left Clelia, stopped short as it were before another image that replaced it in his mind. He had remembered the Countess of Montevesso. He knew her of old, by sight and reputation. He had seen her no further back than last night by the side of the old Marquis’s chair. Now he had seen the Count de Montevesso himself, he could well believe all the stories of a lifelong jealousy. The doctor’s hard, active eyes stared fixedly at the truth. It was not because of that little savage that gloomy self-tormenting ass of a drill sergeant to an Indian prince wanted young Latham removed from Genoa. Oh, dear no. That wasn’t it at all. It was much more serious.

Before he walked out of the empty dining room Doctor Martel concluded that it would be perhaps just as well for young Latham not to linger too long in Genoa.

===Chinese===
医生在科斯莫走后约莫一个钟头（但不是从同一道楼梯）离开了府邸，在回客栈的路上，他由着自己的愤懑尽情发泄。这头蠢笨的畜生难道以为，他对自己的效劳有什么理所当然的索求权吗？啊，他要那只花花公鸡搬出热那亚！好哇！关他什么事？莫非还要指望他去安排一桩干净利落的小小暗杀，来讨那个一本正经的木头蠢货的欢心？医生对自己重要性的感觉，受到了极大的伤害。纵使睡了一夜好觉，到了第二天上午，他仍未能甩掉这个印象。不过，他总算讲理，并不把这件他私下认作平生所遇最难以置信的冒犯之事，算到科斯莫头上一丝一毫。他只是在科斯莫来吃午饭时，带着一种尖酸的兴味打量他——把他看作那个竟能在那个古怪的小蛮女原始的灵魂里唤起一场爱情之火的生灵。饭吃下来，医生似乎注意到，他这位年轻的同乡身上起了某种变化。他暗暗观察着他。自昨晚以来他出了什么事？他总不至于自己也被那小蛮女迷住了吧——那个在任何情况下都无法调教得配在英国人家做女佣的小蛮女。

科斯莫把他独自留在餐厅里之后，医生的身子仍旧懒洋洋地瘫在椅子上，他的心思则继续绕着那桩好笑的事情打转，那桩你分不清是一见钟情、还是某种遗传性癫狂发作的事情。一个相当英俊的年轻人。也不寻常，气度上颇为出众。整体说来，是一个同胞里完全可以引以为豪的样品，医生进一步沉吟道——他的鉴赏力，是与形形色色的人长久交往所培养出来的。颇具特征的是，他也在那一刻里，以他自己那种乖戾而轻蔑的方式，替那个头发蓬乱、长着钩鼻、双颊烧得通红、两条精瘦的光胳膊像棍子的小蛮女，生出一丝怜悯。医生是个有人情味的人。他名望的根源，正出自这份人情味。可他的心思一离开克莱莉娅，便仿佛在另一个取代了她的形象面前戛然止住。他想起了蒙特韦索伯爵夫人（Countess of Montevesso）。他早就认得她——见过的，也听说过。不多不少，就在昨夜，他还见过她坐在老侯爵的椅子旁边。如今他亲眼见到了蒙特韦索伯爵其人，对那些关于伯爵终生嫉妒的种种传闻，他便能全然相信了。医生那双冷峻而敏锐的眼睛，直勾勾地盯着真相。那个阴郁、自我折磨的蠢驴——一个印度亲王手下的操练军曹——想把小莱瑟姆撵出热那亚，并不是为了那个小蛮女。啊，断然不是。根本不是那么回事。事情要严重得多。

在走出那间空荡荡的餐厅之前，马泰尔医生下了结论：小莱瑟姆若不在热那亚久留，或许是再好不过的事。

### II / II

===Original===
Cosmo, having returned to his room, sat down again at the writing table: for was not this day to be devoted to correspondence? Long after the shade had invaded the greater part of the square below he went on, while the faint shuffle of footsteps and the faint murmur of voices reached him from the pavement like the composite sound of agitated insect life that can be heard in the depths of a forest. It required all his courage to keep on, piling up words which dealt exclusively with towns, roads, rivers, mountains, the colours of the sky. It was like labouring the description of the scenery of a stage after a great play had come to an end. A vain thing. And still he travelled on. Having at last descended into the Italian plain (for the benefit of Henrietta), he dropped his pen and thought: “At this rate I will never arrive in Genoa.” He fell back in his chair like a weary traveller. He was suddenly overcome by that weary distaste a frank nature feels after an effort at concealing an overpowering sentiment.

But had he really anything to conceal? he asked himself.

Suddenly the door flew open and Spire marched in with four lighted candles on a tray. It was only then that Cosmo became aware how late it was. “Had I not better tear all this up?” he thought, looking down at the sheets before him.

Spire put two candlesticks on the table, disposed the two others, one each side of the mantelpiece, and was going out.

“Wait!” cried Cosmo.

It was like a cry of distress. Spire shut the door quietly and turned about, betraying no emotion. Cosmo seized the pen again and concluded hastily:

I have been in Genoa for the last two days. I have seen Adèle and the Marquis. They send their love. You shall have lots about them in my next. I have no time now to tell you what a wonderful person she has become. But perhaps you would not think so.

===Chinese===
科斯莫回到自己房里，又在那张书桌前坐下：因为这一天不是要专门用来写信的吗？暮色早已侵吞了下方广场的大半，他却还在写下去；人行道上隐约的脚步拖沓声、隐约的人声絮语传到他耳里，如同森林深处可以听见的那种惊扰不安的虫豸生命所发出的混响。他需要拿出全部勇气才能继续，把一个个只涉及城镇、道路、河流、山脉、天色的词语堆叠起来。这好比在一出大戏落幕之后，还要苦心孤诣地去描摹那舞台上的布景。一桩徒劳的事。可他还是一路走了下去。待到他终于（为亨利埃塔（Henrietta）的缘故）下到了意大利的平原之上，他才放下笔，心想：「照这样下去，我永远也到不了热那亚。」他像一名疲惫的旅人那样，向后倒进椅子里。他忽然被那种疲惫的厌恶攫住——那是一个爽直的性子，在用力遮掩某种压倒一切的情感之后，所感到的厌恶。

可他果真有什么要遮掩的吗？他自问。

忽然门一下子开了，斯帕尔端着一只托盘，上面摆着四支点亮的蜡烛，大步走了进来。直到这时科斯莫才发觉天已多晚。「我是不是该把这些全撕了？」他望着面前那几页纸，心想。

斯帕尔把两支烛台放在桌上，又把另外两支分置在壁炉两侧，便要出去。

「等一下！」科斯莫喊道。

那像是一声呼救。斯帕尔轻轻把门带上，转过身来，不动声色。科斯莫重又抓起笔，匆匆地收了尾：

我已在热那亚停留了两日。我见到了阿黛勒和侯爵。他们向你问好。下次信里我会多写些他们的事。这会儿我没工夫告诉你她已变成了一个多么了不起的人。不过你也许并不以为然。

===Original===
After he had signed it the thought struck him that there was nothing about Napoleon in his letter. He must put in something about Napoleon. He added a P.S.:

You can form no idea of the state of suspense in which all classes live here from the highest to the lowest, as to what may happen next. All their thoughts are concentrated on Bonaparte. Rumours are flying about of some sort of violence that may be offered to him, assassination, kidnapping. It’s difficult to credit it all, though I do believe that the Congress in Vienna is capable of any atrocity. A person I met here suggested that I should go to Livorno. Perhaps I will. But I have lost, I don’t know why, all desire to travel. Should I find a ship ready to sail for England in Livorno, I may take passage in her and come home at once by sea.

Cosmo collected the pages, and while closing the packet asked himself whether he ought to tell her that. Was it the fact that he had lost all wish to travel? However, he let Spire take the packet to the post and during the man’s absence took a turn or two in the room. He had got through the day. Now there was the evening to get through somehow. But when it occurred to him that the evening would be followed by the hours of an endless night, filled by the conflict of shadowy thoughts that haunt the birth of a passion, the desolation of the prospect was so overpowering that he could only meet it with a bitter laugh. Spire, returning, stood thunderstruck at the door.

“What’s the matter with you? Have you seen a ghost?” asked Cosmo, who ceased laughing suddenly and fixed the valet with distracted eyes.

“No, sir, certainly not. I was wondering whether you hadn’t better dine in your room.”

“What do you mean? Am I not fit to be seen?” asked Cosmo captiously, glancing at himself in the mirror as though the crisis through which he had passed in the last three or four minutes could have distorted his face. Spire made no answer. The sound of that laugh had made him lose his conventional bearing; while Cosmo wondered what had happened to that imbecile and glared at him suspiciously.

===Chinese===
签过名之后，他忽然想到信里只字未提拿破仑。他非得添上几句关于拿破仑的话不可。于是他加了一段附言：

你简直想象不到，此地从最高到最低各阶层的人，都生活在一种悬念之中，不知接下来会发生什么。他们全部的心思都集中在波拿巴（Bonaparte）身上。种种流言四下飞传，说可能有什么暴力要加到他身上——暗杀啦，绑架啦。这一切很难全信，可我确实相信，维也纳的会议什么暴行都干得出来。我在此地遇见的一个人劝我去里窝那（Livorno）。也许我会去。但我已不知道为什么，丧失了一切旅行的兴致。要是在里窝那碰上有一条船正要开往英格兰，我或许就会搭上它，立刻由海路回家。

科斯莫把那几页纸收拢，一边封着信封，一边在心里问自己，那一番话该不该告诉她。他当真丧失了一切旅行的兴致吗？然而，他还是让斯帕尔把信封拿去付邮，趁仆人不在的工夫，在屋里来回走了两圈。这一天他算是熬过去了。眼下又得设法熬过这个晚上。可当他想到，傍晚之后将跟着一个漫长无尽的夜，那夜里满是伴着一种情愫的降生而作祟的种种幽暗念头的相互交战，这前景如此凄凉，压倒一切，他只能报以一声苦笑来应付。斯帕尔回来时，在门口惊得呆若木鸡。

「你怎么啦？你见鬼了吗？」科斯莫问，他忽然止住了笑，用一种心神不定的眼睛盯着那名贴身仆人。

「没有，先生，当然没有。我只是在想，先生是不是在屋里用饭更好些。」

「你这是什么意思？我见不得人吗？」科斯莫刁钻地问，朝镜子里自己瞥了一眼，仿佛方才三四分钟里他所经历的那场波澜能把他的脸扭曲了似的。斯帕尔没有作答。那一声笑让他失却了惯常的镇定；而科斯莫一面纳闷这蠢材出了什么事，一面狐疑地瞪着他。

===Original===
“Give me my coat,” he said at last. “I am going downstairs.”

This broke the spell and Spire, getting into motion, regained his composure.

“Noisy company down there, sir. I thought you might not like it.”

Cosmo felt a sudden longing to hear noise, lots of it, senseless, loud, common, absurd noise; noise loud enough to prevent one from thinking, the sort of noise that would cause one to become, as it were, insensible.

“What do you want?” he asked savagely of Spire, who was hovering at his back.

“I am ready to help you with your coat, sir,” said Spire, in an apathetic voice. He had been profoundly shocked. After his master had gone out, slamming the door behind him, he busied himself with a stony face in putting the room to rights, before he blew out the candles and left it to get his supper.

“Didn’t you advise me this morning to go to Livorno?” asked Cosmo, falling heavily into the chair. Doctor Martel was already at table, and, except that he had changed his boots for silk stockings and shoes, he might not have moved from there all the afternoon.

“Livorno,” repeated that strange man. “Did I? Yes. The road along the Riviera di Levante is delightful for any person sensible to the beauties of Italian landscapes.” He paused with a sour expression in the noise of voices filling the room, and muttered that no doubt Cantelucci found that sort of thing pay but that the place was becoming impossible.

===Chinese===
「把我的外套给我，」他终于说，「我要下楼去。」

这便破了那魔咒，斯帕尔动了起来，重又恢复了镇定。

「楼下那一伙人吵得很，先生。我想您也许不喜欢。」

科斯莫忽然生出一种渴望，想听喧闹——大量的喧闹，毫无意义的、吵闹的、庸俗的、荒唐的喧闹；吵得人无法思想的喧闹；那种仿佛能把人震得失去知觉的喧闹。

「你要干什么？」他粗声粗气地问那个在自己身后逡巡的斯帕尔。

「我正待伺候先生穿上外套呢，先生。」斯帕尔以一种冷漠的口吻说。他受了深深的震动。在主人出去、把门砰地一声摔在身后之后，他板着一张石头似的脸，忙着收拾屋子，然后才吹灭蜡烛，离开屋子去吃自己的晚饭。

「今早不是你劝我去里窝那的吗？」科斯莫问，一屁股重重坐进椅子里。马泰尔医生（Doctor Martel）已经入了座，而且，除了把靴子换成丝袜和便鞋之外，他简直可以当作整个下午都没挪过窝。

「里窝那，」那怪人重复道，「我说过吗？是的。里维耶拉·迪·莱万特（Riviera di Levante）那一路，对任何能领略意大利风景之美的人来说，都是令人心旷神怡的。」他在满屋人声里停了停，面带一丝愠色，嘟囔说，坎泰洛奇（Cantelucci）无疑觉得那类营生很来钱，可这地方正变得叫人没法待下去。

===Original===
Cosmo was just thinking that there was not half enough uproar there. The naval officers seemed strangely subdued that evening. The same old lieutenant with sunken cheeks and a sharp nose, in the same shabby uniform, was at the head of the table. Cantelucci, wearing a long-skirted maroon coat, now glided about the room, unobtrusive and vigilant. His benefactor beckoned to him.

“You would know where to find a man with four good horses for the signore’s carriage?” he asked; and accepting Cantelucci’s low bow as an affirmative, addressed himself to Cosmo. “The road’s perfectly safe. The country’s full of Austrian troops.”

“I think I would prefer to go by sea,” said Cosmo, who had not thought of making any arrangements for the journey. Instantly Cantelucci glided away, while the doctor emitted a grunt and applied himself to his dinner. Cosmo thought desperately, “Oh, yes, the sea, why not by sea, away from everybody?” He had been rolling and bumping on the roads, good, bad, and indifferent, in dust or mud, meeting in inns ladies and gentlemen for days and days between Paris and Genoa, and for a moment he was fascinated by the notion of a steady gliding progress in company of three or four bronzed sailors over a blue sea in sight of a picturesque coast of rocks and hills crowded with pines, with opening valleys, with white villages, and purple promontories of lovely shape. It was like a dream which lasted till the doctor was heard suddenly saying, “I think I could find somebody that would take your travelling carriage off your hands”—and the awakening came with an inward recoil of all Cosmo’s being, as if before a vision of irrevocable consequences.

===Chinese===
科斯莫正想着，这里的喧哗还不及所愿的一半。那些海军军官那天傍晚似乎异乎寻常地沉闷。同一名双颊深陷、鼻子尖削的老上尉，穿着同一身寒碜的制服，正坐在桌子的首席。坎泰洛奇穿着一件下摆长长的栗色礼服，此刻在屋里悄然滑行，不引人注目而又处处留心。他的恩主向他招了招手。

「你知道上哪儿给先生（signore）的马车找一个人、配上四匹好马吗？」他问；坎泰洛奇深深一躬，他便当作肯定的答复，转向科斯莫。「这一路极其安全。乡间到处都是奥地利军队。」

「我想我还是情愿走海路。」科斯莫说，他压根儿没想过要为这趟行程做什么安排。坎泰洛奇立刻悄然滑开去，医生则咕哝了一声，埋头吃他的晚饭。科斯莫绝望地想：「哦，对了，海，为何不走海路呢，远离一切人？」他这些日子一直在路上颠簸——好路、坏路、不好不坏的路，尘土里、泥泞里——从巴黎到热那亚，日复一日在客栈里与形形色色的男女相遇，这一刻，他被一个念头迷住了：由三四个晒得黝黑的水手相伴，在一望可见的蓝色海面上稳稳地滑行向前，沿岸是如画的山岩与丘陵，松林满布，谷地豁然开朗，白色村落点缀其间，还有那形状可爱的紫色海角。那像一场梦，一直持续到忽然听见医生说道：「我想我能找到一个人，把您的旅行马车接过去。」——而觉醒随着科斯莫整个身心的向内一缩而至，仿佛面对着某种不可挽回之后果的景象。

===Original===
The doctor lowered his eyelids. “He is changed,” he said to himself. “Oh yes, he is changed.” This, however, did not prevent him from feeling irritated by Cosmo’s lack of response to the offer to dispose of his travelling carriage.

“There are many people that would consider themselves lucky to have such an offer made to them,” he remarked, after a period of silence. “It is not so easy at this time to get rid of a travelling carriage. Nor yet to have an opportunity to hire a dependable man with four good horses if you want to go by land. I mean at a time like this when anything may happen any day.”

“I am sure I am very much obliged to you,” said Cosmo, “but I am really in no hurry.”

The doctor took notice of Cosmo’s languid attitude and the untouched plate before him.

“The trouble is that you don’t seem to have any aim at all. Isn’t that it?”

“Yes. I confess,” said Cosmo carelessly. “I think I want a rest.”

“Well, Mr. Latham, you had better see that you get it, then. This place isn’t restful, it is merely dull. And then suppose you were suddenly to perceive an aim, such for instance as a visit to Elba—you may be too late if you linger unduly. You know, you are not likely to see a specimen like that one over there again in your lifetime. And even he may not be with us very long.”

===Chinese===
医生垂下了眼皮。「他变了，」他暗自说，「哦，是的，他变了。」然而这并不妨碍他因为科斯莫对出让旅行马车一事毫无反应而心生不快。

「有许多人，若能得到这样一个提议，会觉得自己走运呢。」静默了一阵之后，他说道，「在眼下这个时辰，要脱手一辆旅行马车可不容易。您若要走陆路，想有个机会雇到一个靠得住的人、配上四匹好马，也同样不易。我说的是在像眼下这样、什么事都随时可能发生的时辰。」

「我相信我十分感激您，」科斯莫说，「可我实在并不赶时间。」

医生留意到科斯莫那副无精打采的样子，以及他面前那盘一口未动的菜。

「麻烦在于，您似乎压根儿没有任何目标。是这样吧？」

「是的。我承认。」科斯莫漫不经心地说，「我想我需要歇一歇。」

「那么，莱瑟姆先生（Mr. Latham），您最好还是设法把它弄到手吧。这地方并不叫人安宁，它只不过是乏味罢了。再者，假定您忽然看出了一个目标，比方说到厄尔巴岛（Elba）去走一趟——您要是无故耽搁，说不定就为时已晚。您知道，像那边那一位这样的样品，您这一辈子怕是再见不着第二个了。而且，便连他，也不见得还能跟我们在一起多久。」

===Original===
“You seem very positive about that,” said Cosmo, looking at his interlocutor searchingly. “This is the third or fourth time that I hear that sort of allusion from you. Have you any special information?”

“Yes, of a sort. It has been my lot to hear much of what is said in high places, and the nature of my occupation has given me much practice in appreciating what is said.”

“In high places!” interjected Cosmo.

“And in low, too,” retorted the doctor a little impatiently, “if that is the distinction you have in your mind, Mr. Latham. However, I told you I have been in Vienna quite recently, and I have heard something there.”

“From Prince Talleyrand?” was Cosmo’s stolid suggestion.

The doctor smiled acidly. “Not a bad guess. I did hear something at Prince Talleyrand’s. I heard it from Montrond. You know whom I mean?”

“Never heard of him. Who is he?”

===Chinese===
「您对这一点似乎相当笃定。」科斯莫说，探究地望着与他对话的人，「这已是第三、还是第四次，我从您口中听到这类的暗示了。您有什么特别的消息吗？」

「有，算是有一类吧。命中注定，我听到过许多在高位之上说出的话，而我那营生的性质，又使我颇多品鉴他人言辞的训练。」

「在高位之上！」科斯莫插嘴道。

「也在低位之上。」医生略带不耐地回敬道，「倘若您心里想的是这么一种区别的话，莱瑟姆先生。不过，我告诉过您，我不久前刚在维也纳（Vienna），我在那里有所耳闻。」

「从塔列朗亲王（Prince Talleyrand）那里？」科斯莫木然地猜了一句。

医生尖酸地笑了笑。「猜得不坏。我确实在塔列朗亲王那里听到过一些。我是从蒙特龙（Montrond）那里听来的。您知道我指的是谁吧？」

「从没听说过。他是谁？」

===Original===
“Never heard of Montrond? Oh, I forgot, you have been shut up in that tight island of ours. Monsieur Montrond has the advantage to live near the rose. You understand me? He is the intimate companion to the Prince. Has been for many years. The Prince told somebody once that he liked Montrond because he was not ‘excessively’ scrupulous. That just paints the man for you. I was talking with Monsieur Montrond about Bonaparte’s future—and I was not trying to be unkind, either. I pointed out that one could hardly expect him to settle down if the French Government were not made to pay him the money guaranteed under the Treaty. He could see the moment when he would find himself without a penny. That’s enough to make any human being restive. He was bound to try and do something. A man must live, I said. And Montrond looks at me, sideways, and says deliberately: ‘Oh, here we don’t see the necessity.’ You understand that after a hint like this I dropped the subject. It’s a point of view like another, eh, Mr. Latham?”

Cosmo was impressed. “I heard last night,” he said, “that he is taking precautions for his personal safety.”

“He remembered perhaps what happened to a certain Duc d’Enghien, a young man who obviously didn’t take precautions. So you heard that story? Well, in Livorno you will hear many sorts of stories. Livorno is an exciting place, and an excellent point to start from for a visit to Elba, which would be a great memory for your old age. And if you happen to observe anything remarkable there I would thank you to drop me a line, care of Cantelucci. You see, I have put some money into a deal of oil, and I don’t know how it is, everything in the world, even a little twopenny affair like that, is affected by this feeling of suspense that man’s presence gives rise to: hopes, plans, affections, love affairs. If I were you, Mr. Latham, I would certainly go to Livorno.” He waited a little before he got up, muttering something about having a lot of pen work to do, and went out, Cantelucci hastening to open the door for him.

===Chinese===
「从没听说过蒙特龙？哦，我忘了，您一直被关在我们那个逼仄的岛国上。蒙特龙先生有幸住在那朵玫瑰的近旁。您明白我的意思吧？他是亲王的密友。已经许多年了。亲王有一次对人说，他喜欢蒙特龙，是因为他并不『过分地』讲究操守。这一句话就把这人画出来了。我正与蒙特龙先生谈着波拿巴的前途——我也并非存心要刻薄。我指出，要是由着法兰西政府不按条约规定付给他那笔担保好的款子，就难指望他安分下来。他能看见自己一文不名的那一刻。这就足以叫任何一个人躁动不安。他势必会设法做点什么。我说，人总得活下去。而蒙特龙斜着眼瞧我，慢条斯理地说：『哦，我们这儿可看不出这种必要。』您明白，得到这样一句点拨之后，我就把话头搁下了。这也是一种观点，与别的观点并无不同，呃，莱瑟姆先生？」

科斯莫被触动了。「我昨夜听说，」他说，「他正在为自身安全采取防备。」

「他也许想起了某位当甘公爵（Duc d’Enghien）的下场，一个显然不曾有所防备的年轻人。这么说，您听过那个故事了？那么，在里窝那您会听到许多种故事。里窝那是个令人兴奋的地方，也是动身去厄尔巴岛一游的极好出发点，那将是您晚年的一段珍贵回忆。要是您碰巧在那边观察到什么值得一看的事，劳驾您给我写封短信，寄交坎泰洛奇转。您瞧，我在一笔油的生意上投了些钱，而我不知道怎么回事，世上的一切，连那么一件只值两便士的小事，都受到那个人存在所引起的这种悬念之感的影响：希望、计划、情谊、私情。我要是您，莱瑟姆先生，我一定去里窝那。」他坐了一会儿才起身，嘴里嘟囔着还有一大堆笔墨的活儿要做，便出去了，坎泰洛奇连忙赶去替他开门。

===Original===
Cosmo remained passive in his chair. The room emptied itself gradually, and there was not even a servant left in it when Cosmo rose in his turn. He went back to his room, threw a few pieces of wood on the fire, and sat down. He felt as if lost in a strange world.

He doubted whether he ought not to have called that day at the Palace, if only to say goodbye. And suddenly all the occurrences and even words of the day before assailed his memory. The morning call, the mulatto girl, the sunshine in Madame de Montevesso’s boudoir, the seduction of her voice, the emotional appeal of her story, had stirred him to the depths of his soul. Where was the man who could have imagined the existence of a being of such splendid humanity, with such a voice, with such amazing harmony of aspect, expression, gesture—with such a face in this gross world of mortals in which Lady Jane and Mrs. R.’s daughters counted for the most exquisite products offered to the love of men? And yet Cosmo remembered now that even while all his senses had been thrown into confusion by the first sight of Madame de Montevesso he had felt dimly that she was no stranger, that he had seen her glory before: the presence, the glance, the lips. He did not connect that dim recognition with the child Adèle. No child could have promised a woman like this. It was rather like the awed recollection of a prophetic vision. And it had been in Latham Hall—but not in a dream; he was certain no man ever found the premonition of such a marvel in the obscure promptings of slumbering flesh. And it was not in a vision of his own; such visions were for artists, for inspired seers. She must have been foretold to him in some picture he had seen in Latham Hall, where one came on pictures (mostly of the Italian school) in unexpected places, on landings, at the end of dark corridors, in spare bedrooms. A luminous oval face on the dark background—the noble full-length woman, stepping out of the narrow frame with long draperies held by jewelled clasps and girdle, with pearls on head and bosom, carrying a book and a pen (or was it a palm?) and—yes! he saw it plainly with terror—with her left breast pierced by a dagger. He saw it there plainly as if the blow had been struck before his eyes. The released hilt seemed to vibrate yet, while the eyes looked straight at him, profound, unconscious in miraculous tranquillity.

===Chinese===
科斯莫依旧一动不动地坐在椅中。屋子渐渐空了下来，待到科斯莫起身时，连一个仆人也不剩了。他回到自己房里，往火里添了几块木柴，坐下来。他觉得自己仿佛迷失在一个陌生的世界里。

他怀疑自己今天是不是本该到那座府邸去拜一拜，哪怕只是去道个别。忽然，前一天种种的经过，甚至言辞，一齐袭上他的记忆。那次晨间拜访，那个混血侍女，蒙特韦索夫人（Madame de Montevesso）小客厅里的阳光，她嗓音里的那种诱惑，她那段往事里那种动情的恳求，一一搅动了他灵魂的深处。哪一个男子，能想象世上竟有这般辉煌的人性存在，有着这样的嗓音，有着容颜、神情、姿态如此惊人的和谐——在这粗陋的凡人世界里，在这简夫人（Lady Jane）和 R 太太（Mrs. R.）的千金们竟被算作献给男子的爱情之最精致造物的世界里，竟有这样一张脸？然而科斯莫此刻记起，即便是他初次见到蒙特韦索夫人、全部感官都被搅得一片混乱的那一刻，他也曾隐约觉得她并非一个陌生人，他曾经见过她这份光彩：那份气度，那一瞥，那两片嘴唇。他并未把这一丝隐约的辨识，与孩童阿黛勒联系在一起。没有哪一个孩子，能预示出这样一个女人来。那更像是对一桩预言式异象的、心怀敬畏的追忆。而那是在莱瑟姆庄园（Latham Hall）——却不是在梦里；他确信，没有任何男子，会在沉睡肉体的幽微萌动里，找到过这样一桩奇迹的预兆。那也不是他自己生出的一种异象；那种异象是属于艺术家的，属于受神灵启示的先知的。她一定是某幅他在莱瑟姆庄园见过的画向他预先昭示过的——在那宅子里，人会在意想不到之处撞见画作（多半是意大利画派），在楼梯转角，在幽暗走廊的尽头，在闲着的卧房里。一张光润的鹅蛋脸，映在幽暗的底色上——那高贵的、与真人等高的女子，从狭窄的画框里跨步而出，长长的衣饰由镶宝石的扣子和腰带束住，头上和胸前缀着珍珠，手持一册书和一支笔（抑或是一枚棕榈叶？）而且——是的！他心怀恐惧地看得清清楚楚——她的左乳被一柄匕首刺穿。他清清楚楚地看见它在那里，仿佛那一击就是在他眼前落下的。松开着的刀柄似乎仍在颤动，而那双眼睛直直地望着他，深沉，浑然不觉，带着一种奇迹般的安宁。

===Original===
Terror-struck as if at the discovery of a crime, he jumped up, trembling in every limb. He had a horror of the room, of being alone within its four bare walls on which there were no pictures except that awful one which seemed to hang in the air before his eyes. Cosmo felt that he must get away from it. He snatched up his cloak and hat and fled into the corridor. The hour was late and everything was very still. He did not see as much as a flitting shadow on the bare rough walls of the unfinished palace awaiting the decoration of marbles and bronzes that would never cover its nakedness now. The dwelling of the Grazianis stood as dumb and cold in all its lofty depths as at that desolate hour of the dreadful siege, when its owner lay dead of hunger at the foot of the great flight of stairs. It was only in the hall below that Cosmo caught from behind one of the closed doors faint, almost ghostly, murmurs of disputing voices. The two hanging lanterns could not light up that grandly planned cavern in all its extent, but Cosmo made out a dim shape of the elderly lieutenant sitting all alone and perfectly still against the wall, with a bottle of wine before him. By the time he had reached the pavement Cosmo had mastered his trembling and had steadied his thoughts. He wanted to keep away from that house for hours, for hours. He glanced right and left, hesitating. In the whole town he knew only the way to the Palazzo and the way to the port. He took the latter direction. He walked by the faint starlight falling into the narrow streets resembling lofty unroofed corridors as if the whole town had been one palace, recognizing on his way the massive shape of one or two jutting balconies he remembered seeing before, and also a remarkable doorway, the arch of which was held up by bowed giants with flowing beards, like two captive sons of the god of the sea.

===Chinese===
他像发现了一桩罪行那样惊惧万分，跳将起来，四肢都在发抖。他对这间屋子心生恐惧，惧怕独自待在它那四面光秃秃的墙壁之内——那上面没有任何画，除了那一幅骇人的，此刻似乎就悬在他眼前的空中。科斯莫觉得自己必须离开这里。他抓起斗篷和帽子，逃进走廊。时辰已晚，万籁俱寂。在这座尚未完工、有待大理石与青铜的装饰来遮掩其裸露之处的府邸里，那粗糙光秃的墙壁上，他连一道掠过的影子也看不见。格拉齐亚尼一家（Grazianis）的这所宅子，在它全部高耸幽深的腹内，哑然冰冷，一如在那场可怕的热那亚之围（the siege of Genoa）中那个凄凉的时辰——其时，它的主人饿死在那道大楼梯脚下。也只有在楼下大厅里，科斯莫才从一扇紧闭的门后，捕捉到隐隐约约、几近鬼魅般的争论声。那两盏悬挂的灯笼不足以照亮这座设计恢宏的洞穴般的厅堂的全部，但科斯莫辨出一个朦胧的人影——那名年长的上尉独自靠着墙坐着，纹丝不动，面前放着一瓶酒。等走到人行道上时，科斯莫已经按住了自己的颤抖，稳住了心思。他想要离那所宅子远远的，几个小时，几个小时。他左右望了望，犹豫着。全城之中，他认得的只有去那座府邸的路，和去港口的路。他朝后一个方向走去。他在微弱的星光里走着，星光落进那些狭窄的街道，那些街道像是一条条高耸的、没有屋顶的走廊，仿佛整座城镇就是一座宫殿。一路上他认出了一两个突出的、他记得从前见过的阳台那厚重的轮廓，还认出了一处非同寻常的门道，那门道的拱由两个躬身、长髯飘拂的巨人支撑着，像是海神的两个被掳来的儿子。

### III / III

===Original===
At the moment when Cosmo was leaving his room to escape the haunting vision of an old picture representing a beautiful martyr with a dagger in her breast, Doctor Martel was at work finishing what he called a confidential memorandum which he proposed to hand over to the Marquis d’Armand. The doctor applied very high standards of honour and fidelity to his appreciation of men’s character. He had a very great respect for the old Marquis. He was anxious to make him the recipient of that crop of valuable out-of-the-way information interesting to the French Bourbons which he had gathered lately.

Having sat up half the night, he slept late and was just finishing shaving when, a little before eleven o’clock, there was a knock at his door and Cantelucci entered. The innkeeper offered no apology for this intrusion, but announced without preliminaries that the young English gentleman had vanished during the night from the inn. The woman who took the chocolate in the morning upstairs found no servant ready to receive it as usual. The bedroom door was ajar. After much hesitation she had ventured to put her head through. The shutters being open, she had seen that the bed had not been slept in.﻿ ﻿… The doctor left off dabbing his cheeks with eau de cologne and turned to stare at the innkeeper. At last he shrugged his shoulders slightly.

===Chinese===
就在科斯莫离开自己房间、想要摆脱那幅旧画萦绕不去的幻象——画上是一位美丽的殉道圣女，胸前插着一把匕首——的时候，马泰尔医生（Doctor Martel）正埋头赶完他所谓的机密备忘录，打算把它呈交达蒙侯爵（Marquis d’Armand）。医生在品评人物品性时，把荣誉与忠诚的标准定得极高。他对老侯爵怀有极大的敬意。他急于把自己近来搜集到的一批稀见而珍贵的、令法国波旁王室（the French Bourbons）颇感兴趣的情报，托付到侯爵手中。

他熬夜熬了半夜，醒得迟，刚刚剃完胡子，还差一刻到十一点，就有人敲他的门，坎泰洛奇（Cantelucci）走了进来。这客栈主人并不为自己的擅闯致歉，开门见山地通报：那位年轻的英国绅士夜里从客栈失踪了。早上送巧克力上楼的那个女人，发现并没有仆人像往常那样等着接。卧室的门虚掩着。她犹豫了许久，才大着胆子把头探了进去。百叶窗开着，她看见那张床根本不曾有人睡过。……医生停下往两颊拍古龙水（eau de cologne）的动作，转过身来盯着这客栈主人。末了他微微耸了耸肩。

===Original===
Cantelucci took the point immediately. Yes. But in this case it was impossible to dismiss the affair lightly. The young English signore had not been much more than forty-eight hours in Genoa. He had no time to make many acquaintances. And in any case, Cantelucci thought, he ought to have been back by this time.

The doctor picked up his wig and adjusted it on his head thoughtfully, like a considering cap. That simple action altered his physiognomy so completely that Cantelucci was secretly affected. He made one of his austerely deferential bows, which seemed to put the whole matter into the doctor’s hands at once.

“You seem very much upset,” said the doctor. “Have you seen his servant? He must know something.”

“I doubt it. Excellency. He has been upstairs to open the shutters, of course. He is now at the front door, looking out. I did speak to him. He had too much wine last evening and fell asleep with his head on the table. I saw him myself before I retired.”

The doctor preserving a sort of watchful silence, Cantelucci added that he, himself, had retired early on account of one of those periodical headaches he had suffered from since the days of his youth when he had been chained up in the dungeons of St. Elmo for months.

The doctor thought the fellow did look as though he had had a bad night. “Why didn’t you come to see me? You know I can cure worse ailments.”

===Chinese===
坎泰洛奇立刻领会了他的意思。是的。但这一次，事情却不能等闲视之。这位年轻的英国先生（signore）到热那亚统共不过四十八个钟头多一点。他没有工夫结识许多人。而且不管怎么说，坎泰洛奇心想，他到这会儿早该回来了。

医生拿起他的假发，若有所思地戴到头上，像戴上一顶思索的帽子。这简单的动作把他的相貌整个儿改了，坎泰洛奇暗暗受了触动。他行了一个他那惯有的、古板而恭敬的鞠躬，那样子仿佛一下子就把整桩事都托付到医生手里了。

「你看上去非常不安，」医生说，「你见过他的仆人没有？他一定知道些什么。」

「阁下（Excellency），我对此存疑。他自然上楼去开过百叶窗了。这会儿他在大门口，朝外张望。我跟他说过话。他昨夜酒喝得太多，头搁在桌上就睡着了。我歇下之前亲眼看见过他。」

医生保持着一种戒备的沉默，坎泰洛奇便又补了一句：他自己歇得很早，因为犯了那种周期性的头痛——自从他青年时代被锁在圣埃尔莫堡（St. Elmo）的地牢里一连数月以来，这病就一直缠着他。

医生心想，这家伙看上去倒确实像熬了一夜的样子。「你怎么不来找我？你知道，比这更厉害的症候我也能治。」

===Original===
The innkeeper raised his hands in horror at the mere idea. He would never have dared to disturb His Excellency for such a trifle as a headache. But the cause of his trouble was quite other. A partisan of the revolutionary French from his early youth, Cantelucci had been an active conspirator against the old order of things. Now that kings and priests were raising their heads out of the dust he had again become very busy. The latest matter in hand had been the sending of some important documents to the conspirators in the South. He had found the messenger, had taken steps for getting him away secretly, had given him full instructions the last thing before going to bed. The young fellow was brave, intelligent, and resourceful, beyond the common. But somehow the very perfection of his arrangements kept the old conspirator awake. He reviewed them again and again. He could not have done better. At last he fell asleep, but almost immediately, it seemed to him, he was roused by the old crone whose task it was to light the fires in the morning. Sordid and witchlike, she conveyed to him in a toothless mumble the intelligence that Checca was in the kitchen, all in tears and demanding to see him at once.

===Chinese===
客栈主人一听到这话，惊恐地举起双手。哪怕头痛这种小事，他也绝不敢惊动阁下。可他心烦的，完全是另一桩事。坎泰洛奇从青年时代起便是革命派法国的拥护者，一向是反对旧秩序的积极密谋者。如今国王与教士们正从尘土里抬起头来，他便又忙得不可开交。眼下经手的最新一桩事，是把几份重要的文件送往南方的密谋者手中。他已经找好了信使，安排好了让他悄悄出境的步骤，临睡前最后一件做的事，就是把详尽的吩咐交代给他。那年轻人勇敢、聪明、机警，远过常人。可不知怎的，正是他安排得这般天衣无缝，反倒叫这老密谋者睡不安稳。他把那些布置翻来覆去地复盘。他不能再做得更好了。他终于睡过去了，可他觉着几乎立刻——就被那个老丑婆子推醒了，那老婆子的差事就是早上来生火。她形容委琐，像个巫婆，瘪着没牙的嘴嘟嘟囔囔地告诉他：切卡（Checca）在厨房里，泪流满面，非要立刻见他。

===Original===
This Checca was primarily and principally a pretty girl, an orphan left to his care by his late sister. She was not consulted when her uncle, of whom she stood in awe, married her to the middle-aged owner of a wine-shop in the low quarter of the town extending along the shore near the harbour. He was good-natured, slow-witted, and heavy-handed at times. But Checca was much less afraid of him than of her austere uncle. It amused her to be the padrona of an osteria which in the days of Empire was a notable resort for the officers of French privateers. But on the peace that clientele had disappeared and Checca’s husband, leaving the wine-casks to her management, employed his leisure in petty smuggling operations which kept him away from home.

Cantelucci connected his niece’s irruption with some trouble that men might have got into. He was vexed. He had other matters to think of. He was astonished by the violence of her grief. When she could speak at last her tale turned out to be more in the nature of a confession. The old conspirator could hardly believe his ears when he heard that the man whom he had trusted had committed the crime of betraying the secrecy of his mission by going to the osteria late at night to say goodbye to Checca. She assured him that he had been there only a very few moments.

“What, in a wine-shop! Before all the people! With spies swarming everywhere!”

“No,” she said. It was much later. Everybody was gone. He had scratched at the barred door.

“And you were on the other side waiting to let him in﻿—miserable girl,” Cantelucci hissed ferociously.

===Chinese===
这个切卡，首先而且最要紧的，是个漂亮的姑娘，是她已故的姐姐留给他照管的孤儿。当她那位令她敬畏的伯伯，把她许配给港边沿岸那片下城街区一家酒馆的中年店主时，并没有人征询她的意见。那男人脾气不坏，脑子迟钝，有时下手粗暴。但切卡怕他远不如怕她那严厉的伯伯厉害。当一家酒馆（osteria）的老板娘让她觉得有趣——帝国年代里，这酒馆曾是法国私掠船军官们一处出名的去处。可一旦议和，那帮主顾便销声匿迹了，切卡的丈夫把酒桶交给她打理，自己则把空闲工夫花在些小打小闹的走私上，常常不在家。

坎泰洛奇把侄女的闯入同某些人可能惹上的麻烦联系起来。他恼火得很。他还有别的事要操心。她那悲恸之剧烈叫他吃惊。等她终于能开口说话时，她那一番话与其说是陈述，不如说是忏悔。这老密谋者几乎不敢相信自己的耳朵：他竟听说，他所信托的那个人，犯下了这样一桩罪——深夜跑到酒馆去跟切卡道别，从而泄露了他使命的机密。她向他保证，他在那里只待了极短的片刻。

「什么，在一家酒馆里！当着所有人的面！到处都是奸细！」

「不是的，」她说。那是在很久之后。人都散了。他在那扇上了闩的门外抓挠。

「而你在门那一头等着放他进来——你这该死的丫头，」坎泰洛奇穷凶极恶地咬牙低语。

===Original===
She stared at her terrible uncle with streaming eyes. “Yes, I was.” She had not the heart to refuse him. He stayed only a little moment.﻿ ﻿… (Cantelucci ground his teeth with rage. It was the first he had heard of this affair. Here was a most promising plot endangered by this *bestialita*.)﻿ ﻿… Only one little hug, and then she pushed him out herself. Before she had finished putting up the bar she heard a tumult in the street. Shots, too. Perhaps she would have rushed out but her husband was home for a few days. He came down to the wine-shop very cross and boxed her ears, she did not know why. Perhaps for being in the shop at that late hour. That did not matter; but he drove her before him up the stairs and she had to sham sleep for hours till he began to snore regularly. She had grown so desperate that she took the risk of running out and telling her uncle all about it. She thought he ought to know. What brought her to the inn really was a faint hope that Attilio, having eluded the assassins (she was sure they were assassins), had taken refuge there unscathed﻿—or wounded perhaps. She said nothing of this, however. Before Cantelucci’s stony bearing she broke down. “He is dead﻿—*poverino.* My own hands pushed him to his death,” she moaned to herself crazily, standing in front of her silent uncle before the blazing kitchen fire in the yet slumbering house.

===Chinese===
她泪眼汪汪地盯着她那可怕的伯伯。「是的，我在。」她狠不下心拒绝他。他只待了极短的片刻。……（坎泰洛奇气得直磨牙。这事他还是头一回听说。一桩大有可为的密谋，竟因这等*畜生行径*（bestialita）而岌岌可危。）……只抱了一下，然后是她亲手把他推了出去。她还没来得及把门闩插完，就听见街上一阵骚动。还有枪声。她原本会冲出去的，可她丈夫那几日正在家里。他气冲冲地下到酒馆来，给了她一记耳光，她也不知道是为什么。也许是因为这么晚了她还待在酒馆里。那倒不要紧；可他把她赶到楼上，她只得装睡了好几个钟头，直到他开始匀匀地打起鼾来。她已急得走投无路，便冒着险跑出来，把事情原原本本告诉了伯伯。她觉得他应当知道。她真正到客栈来的缘由，是一线微弱的希望——阿蒂利奥（Attilio）既已躲过了那些凶手（她确信那是凶手），也许安然无恙地——也许带了伤——躲到这里来了。然而这些她一句也没说出口。在坎泰洛奇那石像一般的神色面前，她崩溃了。「他死了——*可怜的孩子*（poverino）。是我亲手把他推向死路的，」她神经错乱地自言自语着呻吟，站在沉默的伯伯面前，站在尚在沉睡的宅子里那熊熊燃烧的厨房炉火前。

===Original===
Rage kept Cantelucci dumb. He was as shocked by what he had heard as the most rigid moralist could desire. But he was a conspirator, and all he could see in this was the criminal conduct of those young people who ought to have thought of nothing but the liberation of Italy. For Attilio had taken the oath of the Carbonari; and Checca belonged to the women’s organization of that secret society. She was an *ortolana*, as they called themselves. He had initiated her and was responsible for her conduct. The baseness﻿—the stupidity﻿—the frivolity﻿—the selfishness!

By severe exercise of self-restraint he refrained from throwing her out into the street all in tears as she was. He only muttered awfully at her, “Get out of my sight, you little fool,” with a menacing gesture; but she stood her ground; she never flinched before his raised hand. And as it fell harmless by his side she seized it in both her own, pressing it to her lips and breast in turn, whispering the while all sorts of endearing names at the infuriated Cantelucci. He heard the sounds of his staff beginning the work of the day, their voices, their footsteps. They would wonder﻿—but his niece did not care. She clung to his hand, and he did not get rid of her till he had actually promised to send her news directly he had heard something himself. And she even thought of the means. There was that fine sailor with black whiskers in attendance on the English officers frequenting the hotel. He was a good-natured man. He knew the way to the wine-shop.

This reminded her of her husband. What if he should wake in her absence? And still distracted, she ran off at last, leaving Cantelucci to face the situation.

===Chinese===
愤怒使坎泰洛奇哑口无言。他所听见的，足以让最刻板的道学家都满意地感到震惊。但他是个密谋者，在这件事里他所能看见的，只有那两个年轻人的犯罪行径——他们本应除了意大利的解放之外什么都不想。因为阿蒂利奥已宣过烧炭党（Carbonari）的誓；而切卡属于那个秘密结社的女性组织。她是他们自称的「女菜贩」（*ortolana*）。是他引导她入会，对她的行为负有责任。何其卑劣——何其愚蠢——何其轻浮——何其自私！

靠着极力克制自己，他才没有把她这副泪人儿的模样扔到街上去。他只是可怕地朝她咕哝了一句：「从我眼前滚出去，你这个小傻瓜，」还做了一个威吓的手势；可她站着不动；在他扬起的手前她毫不畏缩。当那只手无力地垂落到他身侧时，她用双手一把抓住它，轮番按到自己的嘴唇和胸口上，一边对着暴怒的坎泰洛奇低声唤出种种亲昵的称呼。他听见手下的伙计们开始了一天的活计，听见他们的声音、他们的脚步声。他们会纳闷——可他侄女毫不在乎。她紧紧抓着他的手不放，直到他真的答应了一有消息就立刻派人告诉她，她才松开。她甚至想到了送信的法子。住在那家客栈、侍候来访英国军官的，有这么一个留着黑络腮胡的漂亮水手。他是个和气的人。他认得到酒馆的路。

这一来倒提醒了她丈夫。万一她不在的时候他醒了怎么办？她依旧心神不宁，终于跑了出去，留下坎泰洛奇一个人来面对这个局面。

===Original===
He was dismayed. He did not really know what had happened﻿—not to his messenger but to the documents. The old conspirator, battling with his thoughts, moved so silent and stern amongst his people that nobody dared approach him for a couple of hours. And when they did at last come to him with the news of the young “milord’s” disappearance he simply swore at them. But as the morning advanced he came to the conclusion that for various reasons it would be best for him to seek his old benefactor. He did so with a harassed face which caused the doctor to believe in the story of a sleepless night. Of course he spoke only of Cosmo’s absence.

The doctor, leaning back against the edge of his dressing table, gazed silently at the innkeeper. He was profoundly disturbed by the intelligence. “Got your snuffbox on you?” he asked.

The alacrity of Cantelucci in producing his snuffbox was equalled by the deferential flourish with which he held it out to his benefactor.

“The young English signore,” he remarked, “visited the Palazzo of the Griffins the evening before.”

The doctor helped himself to a pinch. “He didn’t spend the night there, though,” he observed. “You know who lives in the Palazzo, don’t you, Cantelucci?”

“Some Piedmontese general, I understand, Your Excellency,” said Cantelucci, who had been in touch with Count Helion ever since the Austrian occupation, and had even forwarded secretly one or two letters for the Count to Elba. But these were addressed to a grain merchant in Porto Ferraio. “I will open all my mind to Your Excellency,” continued Cantelucci. “An English milord is a person of consequence. If I were to report his disappearance the police would be coming here to make investigation. I don’t want any police in my house.”

The doctor lost his meditative air. “I daresay you don’t,” he said grimly.

“I recommend myself to Your Excellency’s protective influence,” murmured Cantelucci insinuatingly.

The doctor let drop the pinch of snuff between his thumb and finger. “And he may have come back while we are talking here,” he said hopefully. “Go down, Cantelucci, and send me my courier.”

===Chinese===
他大为沮丧。他其实并不清楚出了什么事——不是他的信使出了什么事，而是那些文件。这老密谋者与自己的念头苦斗着，在手下的伙计们中间走动，沉默而严峻，竟一连两个钟头没人敢近他的身。等到他们终于来向他报告那位年轻「老爷」（milord）失踪的消息时，他干脆把他们臭骂了一顿。但随着上午渐渐过去，他得出结论：出于种种缘故，他最好还是去找他这位老恩主。他一脸憔悴地来了，那模样倒让医生相信了他彻夜未眠的说法。当然，他只字未提别的，只说科斯莫不见了。

医生身子靠着梳妆台的边沿，默默地凝视着这位客栈主人。这消息使他深感不安。「你身上带鼻烟壶了吗？」他问。

坎泰洛奇掏鼻烟壶的麻利劲儿，与他把鼻烟壶递到恩主面前那恭敬的派头，不相上下。

「那位年轻的英国先生，」他说，「前天晚上去过鹰首狮身兽宫（the Palazzo of the Griffins）。」

医生捏了一撮鼻烟。「不过，他总没有在那儿过夜，」他评论道，「坎泰洛奇，你知道住在那宫里的是谁吧？」

「阁下，我听说是什么皮埃蒙特的将军，」坎泰洛奇说——其实自奥地利人占领以来，他一直与海利翁伯爵（Count Helion）有往来，甚至曾秘密替伯爵转交过一两封寄往厄尔巴岛（Elba）的信。可那些信的收件人都是费拉约港（Porto Ferraio）的一位谷物商人。「阁下，我向阁下和盘托出，」坎泰洛奇接着说，「一位英国老爷是个要紧人物。要是我去报告他失踪的事，警察就会到这儿来调查。我可不愿我家里招来警察。」

医生收起了那副若有所思的神气。「我想你是不愿意的，」他冷冷地说。

「小可这就仰仗阁下的庇护了，」坎泰洛奇谄媚地低声说。

医生让拇指与食指间那一撮鼻烟落了下来。「再说了，没准儿咱们在这儿说话这工夫，他已经回来了呢，」他抱着希望地说，「下去吧，坎泰洛奇，把我的跟班给我叫来。」

===Original===
But the doctor’s man was already at the door, bringing the brushed clothes over his arm. While dressing, the doctor speculated on the mystery. It baffled all his conjectures. A man may go out in the evening for a breath of fresh air and get knocked on the head. But how unlikely! He spoke casually to his man who was ministering to him in gloomy silence.

“You will have to step over to the police presently and find out whether anything has happened last night. Do it quietly.”

“I understand,” said the courier surlily. The thought that the fellow had been drunk recently crossed the doctor’s mind.

“Whom were you drinking with last night?” he asked sharply.

“The English servant,” confessed the courier-valet grumpily. “His master let him off his services last night.”

“Yes. And you made him pay the shot.” With these words the doctor left the room. While crossing the great hall downstairs he had the view of Spire’s back framed in the entrance doorway. The valet had not apparently budged from there since seven. So Mr. Latham had not returned. In the dining room there were only two naval officers at the table reserved for them: the elderly gentleman in his usual place at the head, and a round-faced florid person in a bobbed wig, who might have been the ship’s surgeon. During their meal the doctor did not hear them exchange a single remark. They went away together, and after the last of the town customers had left the room, too, the doctor sat alone before his table, toying with a half-empty glass thoughtfully. His grave face was startlingly at variance with the short abrupt laugh which he emitted as he rose, pushing his chair back. It was provoked by the thought that only last evening he had been urging half jestingly his young countryman to leave Genoa in one of the conventional ways, by road or sea, and now he was gone with a vengeance﻿—spirited away, by Jove! The doctor was startled at the profound change of his own feelings. Count Helion’s venomous, “I don’t want that popinjay here” did not sound so funny in his recollection now. Very extraordinary things could and did happen under the run of everyday life. Was it possible that the word of the riddle could be found there? he asked himself.

===Chinese===
可医生自己的跟班已经到了门口，臂弯里搭着刷好的衣服。一边穿衣，医生一边琢磨这桩谜案。它叫一切揣测都落了空。一个人晚上出去透口气，挨了当头一棒，这是可能的。可这未免太不可能！他随口对那个阴沉着脸、一言不发地伺候他的跟班说道：

「你待会儿得去一趟警察局，问清楚昨夜是否出了什么事。这事要悄悄地办。」

「明白。」那跟班没好气地说。一个念头掠过医生脑际——这家伙近来喝醉过。

「昨夜你跟谁喝酒来着？」他厉声问。

「那个英国仆人，」跟班兼贴身仆人老大不情愿地供认，「他主人昨夜免了他的差使。」

「是啊。然后你让他付了酒钱。」说完这话，医生便走出了房间。穿过楼下的大厅时，他望见斯帕尔（Spire）的背影正框在大门口。这贴身仆人从七点钟起显然就没挪过窝。这么说，莱瑟姆先生（Mr. Latham）没有回来过。餐厅里只有两位海军军官坐在他们预留的桌前：那位年长的绅士照旧坐在上首，另一位是个圆脸红扑扑、戴一顶短假发的人物，大概是船上的军医。他们用餐时，医生没听见他们交谈过一个字。两人一同走了，等到城里来的最后一位食客也离了席，医生便独自坐在自己的桌前，若有所思地把玩着一只半空的酒杯。他那张凝重的脸上，忽然发出一声短促而突兀的笑，两者反差之鲜明令人吃惊——他这一笑，是站起身把椅子往后一推时迸出来的。惹他发笑的，是这样的念头：就在昨天傍晚，他还半开玩笑地力劝他这位年轻的同胞，按走陆路或海路的常情离开热那亚，而眼下他倒好，果真一走了之了——凭空不见了，老天爷！医生自己也吃惊于自己心情的这般深刻变化。海利翁伯爵那句恶狠狠的「我不要那个花花公鸡待在这儿」，此刻回想起来，竟一点也不觉得好笑了。在日常生活的寻常进程底下，极不寻常的事是可能、也确实发生着的。这谜底，莫非真能在那底下找到？他暗自问自己。

===Original===
This investigator of the secret discontents and aspirations of his time had never shut his ears to the mere social gossip that came in his way. He had lived long, he remembered much. For instance, he could remember things that were said about Sir Charles Latham long before Cosmo was born. As to the story of the Montevesso marriage, that had made noise enough in its time in society and also amongst the French émigrés. Its celebration, the subsequent differences, reconciliations, recriminations, and final arrangement had kept idle tongues wagging for years. Of course it was that match which had given that dubious Montevesso his social standing; and what followed had invested that absurd individual with the celebrity of a character out of a Molière comedy: *Le Jaloux*. The elderly jealous husband. Comic enough. But that was the sort of comedy that soon takes a tragic turn. A special provocation, a sudden opportunity are enough. What puzzled the doctor was the suddenness of the problem. Yet one could not tell what an orientalized brute, no stranger probably to palace murders, had not the means of doing. He might have been harbouring in that barn of a palace some retainers of a deadly kind. A Corsican desperado, or a couple of rascals from his own native mountains. Had he not two unattractive old peasant women concealed there?

The doctor believed that unlikely things happened every day. This view was not the result of inborn credulity but of much acquired knowledge of a secret sort. A serious, fastidious, and obviously earnest-minded young man, like Latham, was particularly liable to get into trouble of a grave kind. A manifestation of perfectly innocent sympathy could do it, and even less. An unguarded glance. An unconscious warmth of tone. Confound it! Yet he could not let a young countryman of his, a nice, likable young gentleman, vanish from under his nose without taking some steps.

===Chinese===
这位调查他那时代隐秘的不满与抱负的人，从不曾堵上自己的耳朵，不去听那些凑到他面前的纯粹社交流言。他活得很久，记得的很多。譬如，他能记得在科斯莫出生很久以前，关于查尔斯·莱瑟姆爵士（Sir Charles Latham）的种种传闻。至于蒙特韦索那桩婚事，当年在社交场上、在法国流亡贵族（émigré）中间，闹得够喧腾的了。那婚事的操办，随后的龃龉、和解、相互指责，以及最后的安排，一连数年让闲人们的舌头不得消停。当然，正是那桩婚事，给了那个来路不明的蒙特韦索以社交地位；其后所发生的一切，又让这个荒唐人物染上了一层莫里哀（Molière）喜剧人物般的声名：《妒夫》（*Le Jaloux*）。年老的、妒忌的丈夫。够滑稽的。可这正是那种很快就要转为悲剧的喜剧。一次特别的挑衅，一个突如其来的机会，便已足够。让医生困惑的是这桩事的突如其来。然而，谁也说不上一个东方化了的粗胚——大概对宫廷谋杀也并不陌生——没有什么做不到的事。他也许在那谷仓般的宫殿里，豢养着几个要人命的爪牙。一个科西嘉（Corsican）亡命徒，或者从他自己老家山里来的一两个无赖。他不是在那宫里藏着两个其貌不扬的农妇模样的老太婆吗？

医生相信，不大可能的事每天都在发生。这一看法并非出自天生的轻信，而是来自大量秘而不宣的见闻。一个严肃、挑剔、显然心思端正的年轻人，像莱瑟姆这样的，尤其容易卷入严重的麻烦。一次完全无辜的同情表示便足以惹祸，甚至更少。一个不慎的眼神。一个无意识的口气发热。真见鬼！可他总不能眼睁睁看着自己一位年轻的同胞，一个体面、讨人喜欢的年轻绅士，就在他鼻子底下消失，而不采取任何步骤。

===Original===
The doctor stepped out into the hall, attractively dim and cool in the middle of the day. Spire had disappeared, but the doctor had given up the hope of Cosmo’s return. In a dark corner he perceived the shadowy shape of a cocked hat, and made out the old lieutenant leaning back against the wall with his arms crossed and his chin on his breast. He had a bottle of wine and a glass standing in front of him.

“I suppose,” thought the doctor, “this is what he comes ashore for.”

The product of twenty years of war. The reeking loom that converted such as he into food for guns had stopped suddenly. There would be no demand for heroes for a long, long time, and somehow the fact that the fellow had all his limbs about him made him even more pathetic. The doctor had almost forgotten Cosmo. He did not notice Spire coming down the stairs, and he started at the sound of the words, “I beg your pardon, sir,” uttered almost in his ears. The elderly valet was very much shaken. He said in a low murmur, “I am nearly out of my mind, sir. My master﻿ ﻿…”

“I know,” interrupted the doctor. He pounced upon Spire like a bird of prey. “Come, what do you know about it?”

This reception roused Spire’s dislike of that sour and offhand person like no medical man he had ever seen and certainly no gentleman. On the principle, “like master like man,” Spire was more sensitive to manner than to any trait of personality. He pulled himself together and steadied his voice. “I know nothing, sir, except that you were the last person seen speaking to Mr. Latham.”

“You don’t think I have got him in my pocket, do you?” asked Doctor Martel, noting the hostile stare. “Don’t you attend your master when he retires for the night?”

===Chinese===
医生步入大厅，正午时分的大厅有一种诱人的幽暗与清凉。斯帕尔不见了，但医生早已放弃了科斯莫会回来的指望。在一个幽暗的角落里，他辨出一顶三角帽的朦胧轮廓，进而辨出那位年迈的上尉——他向后靠着墙，双臂交叉，下巴垂在胸前。他面前摆着一瓶酒和一只杯子。

「我猜，」医生心想，「他上岸来为的就是这个。」

二十年战争的产物。那台把他们这种人变成炮灰的、血腥气熏天的织机，戛然停下了。在很长很长一段时间里，都不会再需要英雄了，而不知怎的，这家伙居然四肢齐全、毫发无损，这反倒使他更显可怜。医生几乎把科斯莫忘了。他没有注意到斯帕尔正从楼梯上下来，等那句「恕我冒犯，先生」几乎就在他耳边响起时，他吃了一惊。这位年迈的贴身仆人深受震动。他低声咕哝道：「我快要疯了，先生。我家主人……」

「我知道。」医生打断他。他像一头猛禽般扑向斯帕尔。「说吧，这事你知道些什么？」

这番接待激起了斯帕尔对这个乖戾、倨傲之人的反感——他从没见过这样不像医生的人，而且肯定也不是个绅士。本着「有其主必有其仆」的原则，斯帕尔对举止做派的敏感，远胜于对任何性情人品的敏感。他镇定下来，稳住嗓音。「我什么也不知道，先生，我只知道，最后一个被看见跟莱瑟姆先生说话的人，是您。」

「你总不至于以为我把他揣在口袋里了吧？」马泰尔医生问，留意到对方敌意的目光。「你家主人夜里就寝时，你不侍候他吗？」

===Original===
“I got dismissed early last night. I am sorry to say I sat downstairs after supper very late, listening to tales about one thing and another. I﻿ ﻿… I went to sleep there,” added Spire with a sort of desperation.

“Listening to tales,” repeated the doctor jeeringly. “Pretty tales they must have been, too. Zillers is no company for a respectable English servant. You ought to be ashamed of yourself. Well, and then?”

“I went up, sir, and﻿ ﻿…”

“In the middle of the night,” suggested the doctor.

“It was pretty late. I﻿ ﻿…”

He faltered at the remembrance. The waking up in the cold dark kitchen, the cold dark staircase, the light shining through the keyhole of Mr. Cosmo’s bedroom, the first vague feeling that there was something wrong, the empty room. And most awful of all, the bed not slept in, and the candles in the candelabras burning low. He remembered his horror, incredulity, his collapse into an armchair where he sat till broad daylight in a pitiable state of mental agitation.

A slight tremor passed through his portly frame before he forced himself to speak.

“Mr. Latham had emptied his pockets, sir, as if he were making ready to go to bed. All the change and the keys were lying on the mantelpiece. One would think he had been kidnapped. Of course it can’t be,” he added in a low, intense tone.

“Do you mean to say he disappeared without his hat?” asked the doctor.

“No, sir, hat and cloak aren’t there.” And to the doctor’s further questions Spire confessed that he had spoken to no one in the house that morning. He would only have been told lies. He did not think much of the people in the inn.

“So I took the liberty of speaking to you, sir. Mr. Latham may turn up any moment and I don’t know that he would like to find that I have been to the police already.”

“No, perhaps he wouldn’t,” assented the doctor reflectively.

“That’s just it, sir,” murmured Spire. “Mr. Cosmo is a very peculiar young gentleman. He doesn’t like notice to be taken.”

“Doesn’t he? Well then, you had better wait before you go to the police. We had better give him till four o’clock.”

“Very well, sir,” said Spire, fighting down his feeling that nothing in the world would be worse than this waiting. The doctor nodded dismissal, then at the last moment:

“By the by, hadn’t you better look up all the papers that may be lying about?”

Spire was favourably impressed by the suggestion.

“Yes, sir, we have a small strong box with us. I will go and do it at once.”

===Chinese===
「昨夜主人早早地就免了我的差使。我很惭愧地说，晚饭后我在楼下坐到很晚，听人讲这样那样的故事。我……我竟在那儿睡着了，」斯帕尔带着一种绝望的神气补充道。

「听人讲故事，」医生讥诮地重复，「那一定是很精彩的故事。齐勒斯可不是体面英国仆人该结交的伴儿。你该害臊才是。嗯，后来呢？」

「我上楼去了，先生，接着……」

「半夜三更的，」医生提示道。

「确实挺晚了。我……」

回忆袭来，他结巴起来。在那冰冷漆黑的厨房里醒来，那冰冷漆黑的楼梯，科斯莫先生卧室锁孔里透出的灯光，头一阵隐约觉得事情不对劲，那间空屋子。而最可怕的是，那张床根本没人睡过，烛台上的蜡烛已烧到将尽。他记得自己那一刻的恐惧、难以置信，记得自己瘫倒在一张扶手椅里，一直坐到大天亮，精神极度焦灼，可怜至极。

他壮硕的身躯微微一颤，才逼着自己开口。

「莱瑟姆先生已经把口袋里的东西都掏空了，先生，仿佛是准备就寝的样子。所有的零钱和钥匙都搁在壁炉台上。任谁都会以为他是被绑架了。当然，这不可能，」他用一种低沉而紧张的口气补充道。

「你是说，他连帽子都没戴就失踪了？」医生问。

「不，先生，帽子和斗篷都不在那儿。」对医生进一步的盘问，斯帕尔承认，那天早上他没跟客栈里任何一个人说过话。他只会被人编一套谎话。他对这客栈里的人评价不高。

「所以我就冒昧地来找您了，先生。莱瑟姆先生没准儿随时都会出现，我不知道他若是发觉我已经去过警察局，会不会高兴。」

「不会，他大概不高兴。」医生若有所思地附和道。

「正是如此，先生，」斯帕尔低声说，「科斯莫先生是个非常古怪的年轻绅士。他不喜欢被人关注。」

「不喜欢吗？那么，你去警察局之前最好再等等。我们最好给他宽限到四点钟。」

「很好，先生，」斯帕尔说，一面强压下心里那种「再没比这等待更糟的事了」的感觉。医生点头示意他退下，可到了最后一刻又道：

「对了，你最好把可能散落在外头的那些文件都收起来。」

斯帕尔对这个建议颇有好感。

「是，先生，我们随身带了一只小保险箱。我这就去办。」

===Original===
During that colloquy, conducted in low tones at the foot of the grand staircase, nobody had appeared in the hall. Not even the vigilant Cantelucci. But the elderly lieutenant had raised his head, and his dull uninterested eyes followed the doctor across the hall and out through the door into the sunshine of the square. In all its vast and paved extent only very few figures were moving. The doctor’s tastes and even his destiny had made of him a nocturnal visitor to the abodes of the great. At this time of the day, however, there was almost as little risk of being seen entering the Palace of the Griffins as in the middle of the night. The populace, the shopkeepers, the Austrian garrison, the gendarmes, the *sbirri*, the spies, and even the conspirators were indulging in midday repose. The very team of dapple-gray horses, harnessed to an enormous two-wheeled cart drawn up in the shade, dozed over their empty nosebags. Dogs slumbered in the doorways in utter abandonment; and only the bronze griffins seated on their narrow pedestals of granite before the doorway of the Palace preserved their alert wide-awake pose of everlasting watchfulness. They were really very fine. And the doctor gave them an appreciative glance before crossing the empty quadrangle. He felt the only wide-awake person in a slumbering world. He wondered if he would succeed in getting admitted to the Palace. If not, he confessed to himself, he would be at a loss what to do next. Very disagreeable. He had, however, the memorandum for the Marquis in his pocket as a pretext for his visit.

===Chinese===
这番低声的交谈是在大楼梯脚下进行的，期间没有一个人出现在大厅里。连那警醒的坎泰洛奇也没有。倒是那位年迈的上尉抬起了头，他那双木然、不感兴趣的眼睛，随着医生穿过大厅、走出大门、踏入广场的阳光里。在广场那片辽阔铺石的广阔之中，只有寥寥几个人影在动。医生的趣味、乃至他的命运，都使他成了一个惯于夜间出入门第显赫之家的访客。然而在一天里的这个时辰，被人看见走进鹰首狮身兽宫（the Palace of the Griffins），几乎与深更半夜一样没有什么风险。百姓、店主、奥地利驻军、宪兵、巡警（*sbirri*）、奸细，乃至密谋者们，都在享受正午的小憩。就连那一队套在一辆停在荫凉里的巨大双轮大车上的菊花青马，也对着空空的料袋打起了盹。狗在门口四仰八叉地睡着；唯有宫门之前、蹲在窄窄的花岗岩基座上那几只青铜鹰首狮身兽，依旧保持着它们那警醒、永不松弛的永恒守望之姿。它们实在是非常精美。医生在穿过那空旷的四合院之前，向它们投去一道赞许的目光。他觉得自己是这沉睡世界里唯一清醒的人。他不知道自己能否被获准进入宫内。如果不能，他暗自承认，他便不知下一步该怎样办了。非常棘手。不过，他口袋里那份给侯爵的备忘录，正好充作他来访的由头。

===Original===
All was still without and within; but in the noble anteroom at the foot of the marble staircase he was met by a sight characteristic of the easy Italian ways. Extended face downward on one of the red and gold benches, one of the footmen in shirtsleeves and with his breeches untied at the knees was sleeping profoundly. His dishevelled head rested on his forearm. At an unceremonious poke in the ribs he jumped up to his feet, looking scared and wild. But Doctor Martel was ready for him.

“What’s the matter, my friend?” he asked softly. “Is there a price set on your head?”

The man remained open-mouthed as if paralysed by the caustic enquiry.

“Fetch the majordomo here,” commanded the doctor, thinking that he had seldom seen a more bandit-like figure. While waiting, the doctor reflected that a livery coat was a good disguise. It occurred to him also that in the house of a man having such retainers all sorts of things might happen. This was Italy. The silence as of a tomb, which pervaded the whole house, though nothing extraordinary in the hour of siesta, produced the effect of sinister mystery. The arrival of the sleek Bernard did not destroy that bad impression. The doctor, who had never seen him before by daylight, said to himself that this was no doubt only another kind of villain. On learning that the Marquis had been very ill during the night and that Bernard could not think of taking in his name, the doctor inquired whether Madame de Montevesso would see him on most important business. To his great relief (because he had been asking himself all along how he could contrive to get private speech with the Countess) Bernard raised no objections. He simply went away. And again the dumbness around him grew oppressive to Doctor Martel. He fell into a brown study. This palace, famed for the treasures of art, for the splendours of its marbles and paintings and gildings, was no better than a gorgeous tomb. Men’s vanity erected these magnificent abodes only to receive in them the unavoidable guest. Death, with all the ceremonies of superstitious fear. The sense of human mortality evoked by this dumb palazzo was very disagreeable. He was relieved by the return of the noiseless Bernard, all in black and grave like a sleek caretaker of that particular tomb, who stood before him saying in a low voice: “Follow me, please.”

===Chinese===
里外都是一片寂静；可在大理石楼梯脚下那气派的前厅里，他撞见了一幅足以代表意大利人闲散作风的情景。一名男仆脸朝下趴在一张红金两色的长凳上，只穿衬衫，膝头的马裤绑带松开着，正睡得酣沉。他蓬乱的脑袋枕在自己的前臂上。冷不防肋下被人戳了一指头，他一跃而起，神情又惊又野。可马泰尔医生早有防备。

「怎么了，我的朋友？」他轻声问，「是有人悬赏你的脑袋吗？」

那人张着嘴愣在那里，仿佛被这一句尖刻的诘问钉住了。

「去把总管叫来。」医生命令道，心里想着，比这更像强盗的模样他可不多见。一边等，医生一边暗想，一身仆役号衣倒是绝佳的伪装。他还想到，在一个豢养着这等爪牙的人家里，什么事都可能发生。这里是意大利。整座宅子笼罩着那坟墓般的死寂，虽说在午歇时分并不算反常，却产生出一种阴森神秘的效应。油头滑脑的贝尔纳（Bernard）的到来，也并未驱散这种坏的印象。医生从前没在白天见过他，暗自说，这无疑不过是另一种恶棍罢了。得知侯爵夜里病得不轻、贝尔纳想都不敢想替他通报时，医生便问，蒙特韦索夫人（Madame de Montevesso）是否肯见他，有极重要的事。使他大大松了一口气的是（因为他一直在纳闷自己怎样才能设法同伯爵夫人单独说上话），贝尔纳并未阻拦。他只是走开了。周围的死寂重又压迫起马泰尔医生来。他陷入沉思。这座以艺术珍品闻名、以大理石与画作与鎏金之辉煌见称的宫殿，说到底不过是一座华丽的坟墓罢了。人们的虚荣竖起这些宏丽的宅邸，只为在里面迎接那位无可回避的访客。死亡，以及它那一整套迷信恐惧的仪节。这座沉默的宫殿所唤起的人终有一死之感，令人十分不快。悄无声息的贝尔纳的回来让他松了一口气——这一回他通身黑衣，神情凝重，活像那座特定坟墓一位油头粉面的看守，站在他面前低声说：「请随我来。」

===Original===
Bernard introduced the doctor into a comparatively small, well-lighted boudoir. At the same moment Madame de Montevesso entered it from her bedroom by another door. The doctor had an impression of a gown with a train, trimmed with ribbons and lace, surmounted by a radiant fair head. The face was pale. Madame de Montevesso had been up most of the night with her father. The Marquis was too ill to see anybody.

The doctor expressed his regret in a formal tone. Meantime he took out of his pocket the memoir and begged Madame la Comtesse to keep it under lock and key till she could hand it over to her father. He was also in possession of information which, he said, would be of the greatest interest to the French court; but he could disclose it only to the French King or to Monsieur de Jaucourt. He was ready to proceed to Paris should the Marquis be impressed sufficiently by the memoir to procure for him a private audience from the King or the minister.

This curt, businesslike declaration called out a smile on that charming face﻿—just a flicker﻿—a suspicion of it. He could not be offended with that glorious being. He felt only that he must assert himself.

“I cannot deal with lesser people,” he said simply. “This must be understood in Paris. I make my own conditions. I am not a hireling. Your father has known me for years. Monsieur le Marquis and I met in other, dangerous times, in various parts of Europe. Each of us was risking his life.”

===Chinese===
贝尔纳把医生引进一间相对狭小却明亮的闺房。几乎与此同时，蒙特韦索夫人从卧室另一扇门走了进来。医生得到的印象是一袭曳地的长裙，缀着缎带与花边，裙上是一颗光华照人的金发头颅。那张脸是苍白的。蒙特韦索夫人昨夜大半时间都在父亲床前侍疾。侯爵病得太重，不能见任何人。

医生用一种客套的口吻表达了遗憾。与此同时，他从口袋里取出那份备忘录，请伯爵夫人（Madame la Comtesse）妥为锁好，待能交到她父亲手里时再交。他还说，自己手中另有一份情报，对法国朝廷将是最饶有兴趣的事；但他只能向法国国王或若库尔先生（Monsieur de Jaucourt）本人披露。只要侯爵认为这备忘录分量够重、肯为他向国王或大臣谋求一次私下的觐见，他便随时愿意启程前往巴黎（Paris）。

这番简慢而公事公办的声明，在那张俏丽的脸上勾出一丝微笑——只是一掠——若有若无的一丝。他对这样光彩照人的存在生不出气来。他只觉得自己必须有所申明。

「我没法和次要的人物打交道，」他平平地说，「这一点在巴黎必须被人明白。我自己开条件。我不是个卖力气糊口的雇仆。令尊认识我已多年。侯爵先生（Monsieur le Marquis）与我是在另一些、危险的年头，在欧洲各地相识的。我们彼此都曾把性命押上。」

===Original===
The Marquis had often talked with his daughter of his past. She had heard from him of a certain agent Martel, a singular personage. Her curiosity was aroused. She said:

“I know. I believe he was indebted to you for his safety on one occasion. I can understand my father’s motives. But you will forgive me for saying that as to yours﻿ ﻿…”

“Oh! It was not the love of absolutism. The fact is, I discovered early in life that I was not made for a country practice. I started on my travels with no definite purpose, except to do a little good﻿—here and there. I arrived in Italy while it was being revolutionized by Jacobins. I was not in love with them either. Humane impulses, circumstances, and so on, did the rest.”

He looked straight at her. This tête-à-tête was a unique experience. She was a marvellous being somehow and a very great lady. And yet she was as simple as a village maid﻿—a glorified village maid. The trials of a life of exile and poverty had stripped her of the faintest trace of affectation or artificiality of any kind. The doctor was lost in wonder. What humanizing force there was in the beauty of that face to make him talk like that the first time he saw her! And suddenly the thought, “her face has been her fortune,” came to him with great force, evoking by the side of her noble unconscious grace the stiff wooden figure of Count de Montevesso. The effect was horrible, but the doctor’s hard gray eyes betrayed neither his horror nor his indignation. He only asked Madame de Montevesso, who was locking up his memoir in the drawer of a little writing table, if it would be safe there, and was told that nobody ever came into the room but a confidential mulatto maid who had been with the Countess for years.

“Yes, as far as you know,” the doctor ventured significantly. With this beginning he found no difficulty in discovering that Madame de Montevesso knew nothing of the composition of the household. She did not know how many servants there were. She had not been interested enough to look over the Palazzo. Apart from the private apartments and the suite of rooms for small receptions she had seen nothing of it, she confessed, looking a little surprised. It was clear that she knew nothing, suspected nothing, had lived in that enormous and magnificent building like a lost child in a forest. The doctor felt himself at the end of his resources, till it occurred to him to say that he hoped that she was not specially anxious about her father. No, Madame de Montevesso was not specially anxious. He seemed better this morning. Doctor Martel was very much gratified; and then, by a sudden inspiration, added that it would be a pleasure to give the good news to Mr. Latham whom he hoped to see this evening.

===Chinese===
侯爵常常和女儿谈起自己的过往。她曾听他说起过一个叫马泰尔的密使，一个殊异的人物。她的好奇心被勾了起来。她说：

「我知道。我相信有一回他的安全还多亏了您。我能理解我父亲的动机。可您会原谅我说一句——至于您的动机嘛……」

「哦！那可不是出于对专制主义的热爱。实情是，我很早就发觉自己不是干乡间行医那块料。我动身上路，并没有什么明确的目的，不过是想这儿那儿地做点善事。我到了意大利，正赶上它被雅各宾派（Jacobins）闹得天翻地覆。我对他们也没什么好感。人道的一时冲动啦、境遇啦，等等，余下的就都顺理成章了。」

他直直地望着她。这一对一的交谈是一次独特的体验。她不知怎的是个奇妙的存在，又是一位极显贵的夫人。然而她又淳朴得像个村姑——一位升华了的村姑。流亡与贫寒的一生磨砺，已把她身上哪怕一丝一毫的矫揉造作都剥除干净。医生惊叹不已。那张脸的美貌里竟有这般柔化人心的力量，能让他头一次见她就这样说话！忽然间，「她的容颜便是她的财富」这一念头强烈地涌上他心头，由着她那份不自知的雍容气度，唤起了蒙特韦索伯爵（Count de Montevesso）那具僵硬木偶般的身影。这效应是可怖的，但医生那双冷硬的灰眼睛，既不流露他的惊骇，也不流露他的愤慨。他只是问蒙特韦索夫人——她正把他的备忘录锁进一张小书桌的抽屉里——东西放在那儿是否妥当；得到的回答是：除了一个跟随伯爵夫人多年的心腹混血女仆之外，谁也不进这间房。

「是吗，就您所知而已。」医生意味深长地冒了一句。有了这一开头，他便毫不费力地察觉到，蒙特韦索夫人对这宅子里的人员构成一无所知。她不知道有多少仆人。她也未曾有兴致把这座宫邸从头到尾看上一遍。除了私人的住处和几间用于小型招待的套房之外，她什么也没看过——她这样承认时，神情略有几分惊讶。显而易见，她什么都不知道，什么也不曾起疑，住在那座庞大辉煌的宅子里，活像一个在森林里走失的孩子。医生觉得自己已是计无所出，直到他灵机一动，说她想必不至于为父亲格外忧虑吧。不，蒙特韦索夫人并不格外忧虑。今早他看上去好些了。马泰尔医生听了大感欣慰；接着，灵机一动，又添上一句：今晚他希望能见到莱瑟姆先生，把这好消息告诉他，倒是件乐事。

===Original===
Madame de Montevesso turned rigid with surprise for a moment at the sound of that name. “You have met Mr. Latham﻿ ﻿…” she faltered out.

“Oh! By the merest chance. We are staying at the same inn. He shares my table. He is very attractive.”

Madame de Montevesso looked no longer as though she expected her visitor to go away. The doctor had just time to note the change before he was asked point-blank:

“Did Mr. Latham tell you that he was a friend of ours?”

He answered evasively that he knew very little about Mr. Latham, except what he could see for himself﻿—that Mr. Latham was very superior to the young men of fashion coming over in such numbers from England since the end of the war. That generation struck him as very crude and utterly uninteresting. It was different, as far as Mr. Latham was concerned. A situation had arisen which would make a little information as to his affairs very desirable.

“Desirable?” repeated Madame de Montevesso in a whisper.

“Yes, helpful.﻿ ﻿…”

The deliberate stress which he put on that word augmented Madame de Montevesso’s bewilderment.

“I don’t quite understand. In what way? Helpful for you﻿—or helpful for Mr. Latham?”

“You see,” said the doctor slowly, “though our acquaintance was short my interest was aroused. I am a useful person to know for those who travel in Italy.”

Madame de Montevesso sank into a *bergère*, pointing at the same time to a chair which faced it. But the doctor, after a slight bow, only rested his hand on its high back. At the end of five minutes Adèle was in possession of all the doctor knew about Cosmo’s disappearance. She sat silent, her head drooped, her eyes cast down. The doctor was beginning to feel restive when she spoke, without looking up.

“And this is the real motive for your visit here.”

===Chinese===
听到那个名字，蒙特韦索夫人一时惊得僵住了。「您见过莱瑟姆先生……」她期期艾艾地说出来。

「哦！纯粹是机缘巧合。我们住在同一家客栈。他和我同桌用饭。他是个很有魅力的人。」

蒙特韦索夫人脸上不再有盼着来访者离去的那种神情了。医生刚刚来得及留意到这一变化，紧接着便被直截了当地问及：

「莱瑟姆先生有没有告诉过您，他是我们的朋友？」

他闪烁其词地答道，他对莱瑟姆先生所知甚少，除了自己亲眼所见的——莱瑟姆先生远胜于战后从英格兰蜂拥而来的那许许多多时髦青年。那一代人在他看来既十分粗稚，又全无趣味。至于莱瑟姆先生，情形便不同了。眼下出现了一种局面，使他很想对他的事情略知一二。

「很想？」蒙特韦索夫人低声重复。

「是的，有所助益。……」

他刻意在那一词上加了重音，更添了蒙特韦索夫人的困惑。

「我不大明白。在哪方面？对您有所助益——还是对莱瑟姆先生？」

「您要知道，」医生缓缓地说，「尽管我们相识不久，我的兴趣已被勾起来了。对于那些在意大利旅行的人，认识我这么个人是很有用的。」

蒙特韦索夫人跌坐进一张扶手椅（*bergère*）里，同时指着对面的一张椅子。可医生只是微微一躬，把手搭在那椅子高高的椅背上。五分钟过去，阿黛勒（Adèle）已尽知医生所知的、关于科斯莫失踪的一切。她默然坐着，垂着头，目光低垂。医生开始感到焦躁，这时她开了口，并不抬眼。

「这才是您此来的真正动机。」

===Original===
The doctor was moved by the hopeless tone. It might have been an attempt to appear indifferent, but, only in a moment, she seemed to have become lifeless.

“Well,” he said, “on the spur of the moment it seemed the only thing to do.﻿ ﻿… There is somebody in the next room. May I shut the door?”

“It’s only my maid,” said Madame de Montevesso. “She couldn’t hear us from there.”

“Well, then perhaps we had better leave the door as it is. It’s best to avoid all appearance of secrecy.” The doctor was thinking of Count Helion, but Madame de Montevesso made no sign. The doctor lowered his voice still more.

“I wanted to ask you if you had seen him yesterday﻿—last night. No? But he may have called without your knowledge.”

She admitted that it was possible. People had been sent away from the door on account of her father’s illness. There had been no reception in the evening. But Mr. Latham would have asked for her. She thought she would have been told. The doctor suggested that Mr. Latham might have asked for the Count. Madame de Montevesso had only seen her husband for a moment in her father’s bedroom the day before, and not at all yet this day. For all she knew he may have been away for the day on a visit in the country. “But I know nothing of his interests, really,” she said in a little less deadened voice.

She could not explain to the doctor that she was a stranger in that house; an unwilling visitor with an unsympathetic host whose motives one cannot help suspecting. Beyond the time she spent by arrangement every year at Count de Montevesso’s country house she knew nothing of his life. What could have been the motives which brought him to Genoa, she had and could have not the slightest idea. She only felt that she ought not to have accepted his pressing invitation to this hired palazzo. But then she could not have come with her father to Genoa. And yet he could not have done without her. And indeed it seemed but a small thing. The alarming thought crossed her mind that, all unwittingly, she had taken a fatal step.

===Chinese===
那绝望的口吻使医生为之动容。那也许本是试图装作无所谓的样子，可只在顷刻之间，她便仿佛没了生气。

「唉，」他说，「一时情急之下，这好像成了唯一可做的事。……隔壁房间有人。我可以把门关上吗？」

「那不过是我的女仆，」蒙特韦索夫人说，「她从那儿听不见我们说话。」

「那么，也许门就这样开着为好。最好别有任何神秘的模样。」医生心里想的是海利翁伯爵，可蒙特韦索夫人毫无表示。医生把声音压得更低。

「我想问您一句，您昨日——昨夜可见过他。没有？可他也许在您不知情的情况下登过门。」

她承认这是可能的。因父亲抱恙，门口已挡过一些来访的客。傍晚并没有举行招待。可莱瑟姆先生应该会求见她的。她想会有人告诉她。医生提议说，莱瑟姆先生也许求见的是伯爵。蒙特韦索夫人前一日只在父亲卧室里与丈夫见了片刻，今日还根本没见过他。就她所知，他也许到乡间访友，整日都不在。「可他的那些事，我真的一无所知，」她用一种稍稍不那么死寂的声音说。

她无法向医生解释，她在这宅子里是个外乡人；一个并非心甘情愿的访客，面对一个毫不同情的主人，而那主人的动机又不能不令人起疑。除了按约定每年到蒙特韦索伯爵乡间别墅度过一段时间之外，她对他的生活一无所知。究竟是什么动机把他带到热那亚（Genoa）来，她毫不知情，也无从揣测。她只觉得自己本不该接受他殷勤的邀请，住进这座租来的宫邸里。可那样的话，她就不能随父亲一起来热那亚了。然而父亲又少不了她。再说，这本似乎也只是桩小事。一个骇人的念头掠过她心头：她全然不知地，已迈出了致命的一步。

===Original===
The doctor, who had quite an accurate notion of the state of affairs, hastened to say:

“After all, I don’t know that this is of any importance. I have heard that Mr. Latham was busy writing all yesterday. If he had come to Italy with some sort of purpose,” he continued as if arguing with himself, “one could﻿ ﻿…” Then sharply: “You couldn’t tell me anything, could you?” he asked Adèle.

“This is the first time I have seen him for ten years.” Madame de Montevesso raised her eyes, full of trouble, to the doctor’s face. “Since we were children together in Yorkshire. We talked of old times. Only of old times,” she repeated.

“Of course﻿—very natural,” mumbled the doctor. He made the mental remark that one did not disappear like this after talking of old times. And aloud he said, “I suppose Mr. Latham made the acquaintance of Count de Montevesso.”

“Certainly.”

“I presume that they had an opportunity to have a conversation together.”

“I don’t think that Cosmo﻿—that Mr. Latham made any confidences to Count de Montevesso.” While saying those words Adèle looked the doctor straight in the face.

He was asking himself whether she could read his thoughts, when she got up suddenly and walked away to the window, without haste and with a grace of movement which aroused the doctor’s admiration. He could not tell her what he had in his mind. He looked irresolutely at the figure in the window. It was growing enigmatic in its immobility. He began to feel some little awe, when he heard unexpectedly the words:

“You suspect a crime?”

The doctor could not guess the effort which went to the uttering of those few words. It was the stunning force of the shock which enabled Adèle de Montevesso to appear so calm. It was the general humanity of Doctor Martel’s disposition which dictated his answer.

“I suspect some imprudence,” he admitted in an easy tone. At that moment he drew the gloomiest view of Cosmo’s disappearance, from the sinister conviction that twenty-four hours was enough to arrange an assassination. “The difficulty is to imagine a cause for it. To find the motive.﻿ ﻿…”

Madame de Montevesso continued to face the window as if lost in the contemplation of a vast landscape. “And you came to look for it here,” she said.

===Chinese===
医生对事态有着相当准确的判断，赶忙说道：

「说到底，我也不知道这有什么要紧。我听说莱瑟姆先生昨日一整天都在忙着写信。如果他到意大利是带着某种目的的，」他接着说，像是在自辩，「人本可以……」随即又锋利地：「您没什么可告诉我的吧，是吗？」他问阿黛勒。

「这是我十年来第一次见到他。」蒙特韦索夫人抬起充满愁苦的眼睛，望着医生的脸。「自从我们小时候一起在约克郡（Yorkshire）那时起。我们谈了谈旧时光。只谈了旧时光，」她重复道。

「当然——很自然。」医生咕哝道。他心里暗想，人总不至于谈了旧时光之后就这般消失。便开口道：「我想，莱瑟姆先生与蒙特韦索伯爵是结识了的吧。」

「当然。」

「我推测，他们有机会在一起交谈过。」

「我不认为科斯莫——莱瑟姆先生对蒙特韦索伯爵吐露过什么心事。」说这话时，阿黛勒直直地望着医生的脸。

他正在自问，她是否能读出他的心思，这时她忽然起身走向窗前，不慌不忙，那举止的优雅引得医生心生赞叹。他无法把她心里那桩事告诉她。他迟疑地望着窗前那个身影。那身影在一动不动之中，正变得难以捉摸。他开始感到几分敬畏，就在这时，他出其不意地听见这几句话：

「您怀疑是一桩罪行？」

医生猜不出她为说出这几个字付出了多大的努力。正是那一击之震骇，使阿黛勒·达·蒙特韦索（Adèle de Montevesso）能显得如此镇定。而马泰尔医生答话时所秉持的，是他性情里那份普遍的人道之心。

「我怀疑的是某种轻率之举，」他以一种轻松的口吻承认道。此刻他从一种阴郁的确信——二十四个钟头已足以布置一桩暗杀——之中，对科斯莫的失踪作出了最阴暗的估量。「难的是想出一个缘由。找出动机。……」

蒙特韦索夫人依旧面对着窗户，仿佛沉浸在一片辽阔风景的凝望之中。「而您是来这儿找它的。」她说。

===Original===
“I don’t think I need to apologize,” he said, with a movement of annoyance like a man who has received a home thrust. “Of course I might have simply gone about my own affairs, which are of some importance to a good many people. My advice to Mr. Latham was to leave Genoa, since he did not seem to have any object in remaining and seemed to have a half-formed wish to visit Elba. I suggested Leghorn as the best port for crossing over.”

It was impossible to say whether the woman at the window was listening to him at all. She did not stir, she seemed to have forgotten his existence. But that immobility might have been also the effect of concentrated attention. He made up his mind to go on speaking.

“His mind, his imagination seemed very busy with Napoleon. It seemed to me the only reason for his travels.” He paused.

“I believe the only reason for Mr. Latham coming to Genoa was to see us.” Madame de Montevesso turned round and moved back towards the *bergère*. She was extremely pale. “I mean Father and myself,” she explained. “He came to see me the day before yesterday in the morning. I invited him to our usual evening reception. He stayed after everybody else was gone. I asked him to. But my father needed me and I had to leave Mr. Latham with Monsieur de Montevesso.”

The doctor interrupted her gently. “I know, Madame. I was in the Palazzo with the Marquis, in the very room, when he sent for your husband.”

“I forgot,” confessed Madame de Montevesso simply. “But Mr. Latham got back to his inn safely.”

“Yes. He was writing letters next day till late in the evening, and seems to have been spirited away in the middle of that occupation. But people like Mr. Latham are not spirited out of their bedrooms by main force. I advised the servant to wait till four o’clock, then I came straight here.”

“Till four o’clock,” repeated Madame de Montevesso under her breath.
